# Session ledger, July 2026

This file is the July page of the build ledger. Entries are added newest-first at
the top, with one exception kept on purpose: the four-session booking arc runs in
session order, because each entry reads against the one before it. The block below
the boundary line is the pre-split history, moved verbatim out of `CLAUDE.md` and
preserved in its original document order. CLAUDE.md carries current-state lines
only.

---

## 2026-07-28 — Swap day: the record, and a repoint that was already made

Base `9d24ac5`. **No code changed.** This session was booked as a repoint plus a
record, and the repoint turned out to be already in the tree, so what shipped is
the record and nothing else. Three documentation files, one commit.

### The repoint was done four commits ago

The brief asked to repoint the SMM wizard's booking step from the Zoho Bookings
URL to the native URL. Two things were wrong with that premise, both worth
writing down because the cutover inventory said the first one out loud and this
session found the second.

The inventory's finding 3, written this morning: **the SMM wizard never pointed
at Zoho Bookings.** Its Book a Call CTA pointed at `/contact`. There was no Zoho
link in the wizard to undo.

Then `9d2db8e`, committed at 11:49 today, retargeted six public CTAs from
`/contact` to `/book/mike/discovery-call`, and `app/smm/enroll/page.tsx` was one
of the six. So by the time this brief ran, the wizard already booked on the
native engine. The correct move was to verify and report that, not to
manufacture a diff. `app/smm/enroll/page.tsx:631` reads
`href="/book/mike/discovery-call"` and did before this session opened.

### What is actually swapped, and what is not

| Surface | Books on | How |
|---|---|---|
| Renewal drip | Native | `RENEWAL_CALENDAR_URL` set by Michael in fox-underwriting |
| SMM enroll wizard | Native | `9d2db8e`, with five other public CTAs |
| Home, About, Services, Apply, footer | Native | `9d2db8e` |
| Client status page | **Zoho** | reads `CONTACT.bookingUrl` |
| Qualification explorer | **Zoho** | reads `CONTACT.bookingUrl` |
| Six portal support pages | **Zoho** | read `CONTACT.bookingUrl` |

The bottom three rows are one constant, `lib/contact.ts:31`, plus four Support
subtitles that still name the vendor in client-visible copy. The swap-day brief
scoped step 2 to the wizard and said nothing else changes, so that constant was
left alone on purpose rather than by oversight. It is the one piece of the
cutover inventory's section A that has not been executed, and it is a
two-line change whenever Michael wants it.

### The two preconditions, and what this session could verify

The brief gated itself on the T-24h reminder arriving and Michael cancelling
through the link, and on `RENEWAL_CALENDAR_URL` being set in the Vercel
dashboard. **Neither is verifiable from this repo and neither was verified
here.** The env var lives in a different project, the Vercel CLI token has
returned 403 three times, and the standing rule from the cron session applies
directly: repo contents are not evidence about Vercel's environment. Both are
recorded as Michael's assertion, which is what they are.

### Retirement

## 2026-07-28 — The booking clock comes home, first brick out of n8n

Base `65c41ad`. The hourly booking jobs now have a trigger that lives in this
repo instead of a workflow nobody can renew. 1163 → **1182 tests** (+19), tsc
clean, `next build` green. One new file at the repo root, one route extended,
no behaviour change to the jobs themselves.

### What was actually broken

n8n `Uc9CoYm4B2XSpN5m` never ran. Not once. It sat `active: false` AND its HTTP
node had no credential bound, so even activating it would have POSTed with no
header and collected silent 401s. Session four wrote that down and left it as two
clicks in the n8n UI. Those clicks never happened, and the n8n subscription is
lapsing, so the honest move is not to rebind it: it is to take the trigger back.

Since the booking engine shipped, no reminder has ever fired on its own and no
stuck calendar write has ever drained. The jobs were correct the whole time. They
had no clock.

### The clock

`vercel.json` is NEW — the repo had never carried one, so there was no house cron
convention to follow and this establishes it. It holds exactly one thing:

```json
{ "$schema": "https://openapi.vercel.sh/vercel.json",
  "crons": [ { "path": "/api/book/cron", "schedule": "0 * * * *" } ] }
```

Nothing else. A `vercel.json` that sets only `crons` leaves framework detection,
build command, and every other project setting exactly where they are, which is
what you want the first one to do. Vercel cron schedules run in UTC and hourly is
hourly, so there is no timezone arithmetic anywhere in this change.

### The auth change, in one line

`authorise()` now returns `'bridge'` for the existing `x-bridge-secret` header or
`'vercel-cron'` for `Authorization: Bearer <CRON_SECRET>`, and a `GET` handler was
added because **Vercel invokes crons with GET, not POST**. Both verbs run the
identical work through one `runJobs`.

Two details that are easy to get wrong and were not:

- **GET was the trap.** The route was POST-only. A `crons` entry alone would have
  produced an hourly 405 that looks, from the Vercel dashboard, exactly like a
  cron that is wired up.
- **The response body is now discarded.** n8n kept the job log; Vercel's cron
  throws it away. So the run's counts now also go to the runtime log as a single
  `book.cron` line carrying via, job, ok, duration, and per-run
  considered/succeeded/failed/skipped/stuck counts. Counts and ids only: never a
  note's free text, never a payload, never a client's name, email, or number. A
  test asserts a stuck row is logged as a COUNT and that its detail string never
  reaches the log line.

### CRON_SECRET: an inference, and the live tick that disproved it

`CRON_SECRET` **is set on the project with Production scope, and the migration
needs nothing from Michael.** That is the answer, and it is worth recording how
close this session came to reporting the opposite.

The project environment could not be read. The Vercel CLI token at
`~/Library/Application Support/com.vercel.cli/auth.json` returns
`403 {"invalidToken":true}` (the third occurrence, after 2026-07-10 and
2026-07-17), and the Vercel MCP exposes **no env-var tool at all**, so
`GET /v9/projects/{id}/env` was the only path and it was shut. From inside the
repo the evidence all pointed one way: `CRON_SECRET` appears nowhere in
`.env.local`, nowhere in the code, and nowhere in the docs except one line about
how the pattern works on **fox-underwriting**, a different project; and this
project had never carried a `vercel.json`, so nothing would have created the
variable. A probe against production with a junk Bearer returned 401, which is
consistent with the variable being absent — and equally consistent with it being
present and simply not matching, because the route gives one shape for both.

Every one of those observations was true. The conclusion drawn from them was
wrong. **The first native invocation authenticated as `via: "vercel-cron"`,
which is only reachable when `process.env.CRON_SECRET` is set AND the incoming
Bearer matches it.** One live tick settled what no amount of repo evidence could.

The lesson is narrow and worth keeping: **repo contents are not evidence about a
Vercel project's environment.** When the token is dead, the honest move is to
label the absence an inference (which it was) and then go get a real signal —
here, the runtime log of the thing actually running.

What the design got right regardless: with `CRON_SECRET` unset the hourly call
would have arrived with no Authorization header and been refused 401, loudly and
visibly, rather than half-authenticated. That was chosen over gating a publicly
reachable route (it must be public, Clerk would 401 the platform before the
handler ran) on the undocumented and unpromised `x-vercel-cron` header. The
failure mode was sound; it just never came up.

### The first native invocation

`23:01:22 UTC, GET /api/book/cron → 200`, on `dpl_8rCjfYg42Z9cWrFMVFD2WLkpVK1L`:

```
book.cron {"via":"vercel-cron","job":"both","ok":true,"ms":84,
  "runs":[{"ran":"reminders","ok":true,"considered":0,"succeeded":0,"failed":0,"skipped":0,"stuck":0},
          {"ran":"calendar-reconcile","ok":true,"considered":0,"succeeded":0,"failed":0,"skipped":0,"stuck":0}]}
```

Both jobs ran, both clean, nothing to do — which is correct, and was confirmed
independently BEFORE the tick by a read-only count against FOXCA rather than by
firing the job (running it to find out would have sent real client mail). The
whole system holds exactly one booking: a public discovery call starting
2026-07-29 13:00 UTC, whose confirmation went out on creation, whose reminder was
already sent at 2026-07-28 12:38 UTC by a by-hand run inside the 23-to-25 hour
window, and whose calendar entry is `written`. So **no booking sits in a reminder
window, and the reconcile queue is empty** — zero rows `pending_retry`, zero stuck.

Note the fire time: `0 * * * *` fired at **:01:22**, not :00:00. Vercel cron
timing is approximate. A minute of drift is the platform, not a fault.

Also verified live on the deployed route, before the tick: an unauthenticated GET
returns 401 (a 405 would have meant the POST-only route was still live and the
whole migration silently dead), a bridge-authenticated GET returns 200 with the
job log, and a wrong bridge secret returns 401.

One unrelated warning rides the same request: `Clerk: Missing
CLERK_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, emitted by `authMiddleware`. Nothing in this change
touches Clerk or `middleware.ts`, and the request returned 200. Flagged for
Michael, not diagnosed here.

### Decisions worth naming

- **Both auth paths stay valid.** The bridge header is the one known to be
  configured and the one a by-hand run uses; it retires when n8n does, not now.
- **The renewal-day plan for `Uc9CoYm4B2XSpN5m` flips from "bind and activate" to
  DEACTIVATE.** This route is the trigger. The jobs are idempotent (proven in
  session two), so if that workflow is ever revived the double-fire is harmless
  rather than dangerous, which is what made taking the trigger back safe to do
  before the n8n question is settled.
- **The bridge secret is still shared with the underwriting sweep.** Session
  four's note that booking wants its own secret still stands, and it is still
  blocked on the same thing: this session may not write env vars either. It is
  now a smaller debt, because `CRON_SECRET` will be booking's own value the moment
  Michael sets it.

### Files

`vercel.json` (new), `app/api/book/cron/route.ts`,
`tests/booking-cron.test.ts` (new, 19 tests: the schedule entry itself, that every
cron path in `vercel.json` resolves to a route file on disk, that the path is
still exempt in `middleware.ts`, the full auth matrix including the
CRON_SECRET-unset refusal, job selection on both verbs, and the log line's
contents).

## 2026-07-27 — Native booking engine, session two of four

Base `7c43745`. A booking now behaves like one: it confirms, it reminds, it can
be moved or cancelled by the person who made it, it lands in Zoho, and a calendar
write that fails gets repaired instead of lost. 1042 → **1078 tests** (+36), tsc
clean, `next build` green. One migration applied live. Committed and pushed.

### The grant landed, and the capability gate proved its worth

Session one shipped with the Graph credential READ-ONLY and event creation as a
capability-gated stub. Michael granted `Calendars.ReadWrite` between sessions. The
roles claim now reads `["Calendars.Read", "Calendars.ReadWrite"]`, and **not one
line of `lib/booking/outlook.ts` changed to make writing start working** — the
gate reads the tenant's own claim, so the code began writing on its next token.
That was the whole argument for gating on the claim instead of hardcoding a stub,
and it held.

The gate stays. It is not scaffolding: if the grant is ever revoked, createEvent
goes back to refusing before it fires, the reason reaches `calendar_detail`, and
the reconcile job stops hammering a wall.

Write capability was confirmed independently before anything was built on it
(create 201, read back, delete 204, verify 404) because a tenant-level grant can
still be blocked by an ApplicationAccessPolicy. It is not.

### What shipped

**Confirmation mail with an ics.** `lib/booking/email-copy.ts` is pure and
copy-gated; `lib/booking/email.ts` sends through Resend from Michael with
reply-to Michael, so a reply reaches a person. `lib/booking/ics.ts` builds the
VEVENT by hand (no calendar library in this repo): `METHOD:REQUEST` with a stable
UID so a reschedule UPDATES the client's calendar entry rather than adding a
second one beside it, `SEQUENCE` rising on every change so a client actually
accepts the update, `METHOD:CANCEL` on a cancellation so the entry is removed
rather than left as a ghost. No conferencing anywhere, asserted by test. Line
folding measures OCTETS and never splits a code point, which is the bug that
works in testing and breaks on the first name with an accent.

**Reschedule and cancel**, off the session-one token: shape-gated, sha256
compared, raw token never sent to the database. Reschedule is an IN-PLACE move,
deliberately — cancel-then-create would mint a new token and break the link
already sitting in the client's inbox. The move re-runs every rule a fresh
booking runs and lands under the same partial unique index.

**Server-side prefill**, replacing session one's id-only limitation. The link
still carries only opaque record ids; the name, email, and number are fetched
server-side from Zoho and rendered into the form. Personal data never touches the
URL or the token payload.

**Zoho linkage.** Tokened bookings write a Note on the Contact and, where there
is one, the Deal. Untokened bookings create a Lead. `createZohoNote` is new —
nothing in the repo created a Note before, that lived in n8n. Consent writes the
four `CASL_*` fields with method `Express`, source, and the exact sentence the
person read. **No Zoho Tasks are created anywhere in this flow.**

**The reminder and reconcile jobs**, plus the hourly n8n workflow that calls them.

### Decisions worth naming

**The cron route reuses `UW_BRIDGE_SECRET`.** This session cannot provision
environment variables, and the alternative — putting `FOXCA_OPERATOR_SECRET` into
an n8n credential — would copy the database's master key into a second system to
solve a scheduling problem. The cost, stated rather than buried: a leak of that
secret now also unlocks these two jobs. The blast radius is bounded — the route
cannot create, move, or cancel a booking, and returns counts and ids, never a
client's details. **Session three should give booking its own secret.**

**Consent is only ever written on a tick.** There is deliberately no false branch
anywhere in `lib/booking/zoho-link.ts`. An unticked box writes nothing: it does
not clear a date, does not set a method to none, does not touch `Email_Opt_Out`.
Absence of consent is not withdrawal of consent.

**The mail and Zoho steps are awaited, not fired and forgotten.** A serverless
function that has already returned may be frozen before background work runs, and
a lost confirmation is worse than a slower confirm.

**A booking is not an obstacle to its own move** — and it exists in two places, a
row and a calendar event. Session two initially excluded only the row, which
silently withheld the slots either side of the client's current time from the one
person entitled to take them. Caught in the live test (23 slots offered instead of
25), fixed by carrying the provider event id through `BusyInterval` and excluding
both. The manage endpoint and the reschedule engine share the same two helpers, so
the page cannot offer a time the server would refuse.

### The live write test

Run end to end against the real calendar, the real Zoho org, and real Resend.

- **Tokened booking**, through the real page with a real prefill link: form
  arrived filled from Zoho, URL carried only ids, calendar event created at the
  right instant with the phone number in the body and the location, Note written
  on the Contact with the `Booking: ` title prefix, and CASL stamped
  `Express` / dated / sourced / with the verbatim consent sentence. **This also
  settled the open question from discovery about whether the Notes `Parent_Id`
  metadata quirk blocks a write. It does not, via the related-list endpoint.**
- **Untokened booking**, to a real inbox: `lead created`,
  `confirmation_sent = true`, calendar written.
- **Reschedule**, through the manage page: row moved, `original_starts_at` kept,
  `rescheduled_count` 1, old calendar event removed and a new one written, mail
  sent.
- **Cancel**: status cancelled with reason and `cancelled_by`, calendar event
  deleted and the id cleared, `calendar_detail` "cancelled, event removed".
- **Reminder guard**, the behaviour the brief calls out: two bookings identical
  except for when they were created. The one created three days ahead was
  reminded and stamped; the one created inside the 24 hour window was
  `considered: 0`. A second run of the job reminded nobody, which is the
  idempotency guard.
- **Reconcile**: a row forced to `pending_retry` was drained on the next run,
  `considered 1, succeeded 1`.
- **Cron auth**: wrong secret 401, missing secret 401, correct secret 200 with the
  structured log.
- **Resend rejects `example.com`** by policy. The first booking's client mail
  therefore failed — and did so honestly: logged, booking unaffected,
  `confirmation_sent_at` correctly not stamped. That was an unplanned but useful
  proof that the mail step cannot fail a booking.

**Cleaned up, verified gone:** 2 calendar events (0 remaining), 1 Zoho contact
(204), 1 Zoho lead (204), 4 booking rows (table empty). The Zoho contact carried
the test Note and the test CASL consent, so both went with it.

### Known residual, for session three

If a calendar create SUCCEEDS at Graph but the response is lost in transit, the
row records `pending_retry` while an event exists, and the next reconcile run
creates a duplicate. Rare, and not reachable through any tested path, but real.
The fix is an idempotency key on the create, or a search-before-create in the
reconcile job.

### Files

Migration `20260727170000_booking_session_two.sql` (13 columns, 8 functions, one
body-only `booking_get` extension). New: `lib/booking/{ics,email-copy,email,
zoho-link,jobs}.ts`, `config/booking.ts`, `app/book/manage/[token]/*`,
`app/api/book/manage/{slots,reschedule,cancel}/route.ts`,
`app/api/book/cron/route.ts`, `tests/booking-{ics,email}.test.ts`. Five middleware
publicRoutes entries.

n8n `Uc9CoYm4B2XSpN5m` "Booking — Reminders & Calendar Reconcile (hourly)",
INACTIVE pending credential binding, registered in the knowledge base master
index §7 (committed locally there; that repo has no remote).

### Distance to session three

Session three is hardening plus the full test checklist. Ready for it: every
route, both jobs, and the whole lifecycle are live-proven. Outstanding for it to
pick up: booking's own cron secret, the duplicate-event residual above, a durable
rate limiter (the current one is in-process and says so), binding the n8n
credential and activating the workflow, and the two-timezone render check.

Guardrails held: no env vars, no vercel commands, no Azure changes, no swap (the
drip keeps its Zoho Bookings link), `/book` still out of public nav, Graph and
Supabase and Resend and n8n only, no Zoho Tasks, copy gate on every client string,
PII discipline (synthetic and disposable only, all deleted).

See `docs/booking-engine-session-one-2026-07-27.md` for the foundation.

## 2026-07-28 — Native booking engine, session three of four

Hardening the edges and scoring the swap gate. Base `7c5eef9`, **1112 tests**
(from 1078, +34), tsc clean, `next build` green. One migration applied live.
No env vars, no vercel commands, no Azure changes, no new vendors, no swap,
`/book` still out of public nav.

### The rate limiter: the open question, decided

Session one wrote "session three's hardening pass is where it gets decided" into
`lib/booking/rate-limit.ts`. The decision, in the file: **sliding windows, two
keys, still in process.**

- **Fixed windows became sliding ones.** A fixed window has a seam a script can
  aim at, and 2x the limit passes across it. `tests/booking-hardening.test.ts`
  encodes exactly that attack and proves the sliding log refuses it.
- **Every surface carries two tiers**, a burst measured in seconds and a
  sustained one measured in minutes or hours, because one number cannot be both
  generous to a person who reloads twice and hostile to a script that never
  stops. Slots 12/10s + 90/10min. Confirm by IP 4/min + 12/hour. Manage 8/min +
  40/hour.
- **Confirm now limits per EMAIL as well as per IP** (3/hour, 6/day), keyed by
  sha256 so no address is ever held in memory, and checked after validation but
  before any write. Rotating IPs through a proxy pool buys a fresh IP budget and
  not a fresh address budget.
- **Refused attempts are not recorded** and the check is all-or-nothing, so a
  burst refusal cannot silently spend the sustained budget and Retry-After tells
  the truth. Both are tested.

**A durable limiter was considered and DEFERRED, with the reason written down
rather than omitted:** the damage it would prevent is already prevented in the
database where it cannot be evaded by hitting another instance (one active
booking per email per event type per day, the partial unique index on
`(agent_id, starts_at)`, the per-day cap). A durable limiter would put a FOXCA
round trip in front of every slots request, the hot path on the public page, to
stop an attacker who is already stopped one layer down. If booking moves to the
edge, the limiter goes with it.

### Eight edge surfaces, one card

`components/booking/BookingNotice.tsx` is the single design-system home for every
dead end. Session two had grown four near-identical hand-rolled centred cards
across two files and this session adds four more, which is exactly how a phone
number goes stale in one of them. The component writes NO copy of its own: every
word is passed in and gated at its call site.

- **Provider outage.** The engine fails closed, so the card says so in those
  words ("we would rather show you nothing than show you a time we cannot keep")
  and hands over both the phone and the email.
- **No open times.** A different sentence from the outage, because the calendar
  read WORKED and came back empty. Both ways out, where session two offered only
  a phone number.
- **`/book` not found** (`app/book/not-found.tsx`, new). A bad host slug, a
  retired event type, or a link that lost a character used to fall through to the
  framework's bare 404, which is a dead end on a page whose whole job is to start
  a conversation.
- **The manage page now tells four states apart.** A token that does not RESOLVE
  still gets one identical card, and that vagueness is load bearing (the page is
  not an oracle for guessing tokens). The other three are distinguished, and
  distinguishing them leaks nothing because the token already resolved: the call
  already happened, already cancelled, closed out some other way.
- **Too close to change online** keeps the appointment ON SCREEN and swaps only
  the controls, because hiding the details of a call happening in ninety minutes
  is the one thing that would actually make someone late.

### Accessibility

Every form field now carries `aria-invalid`, an `aria-describedby` that points at
BOTH its hint and its error, and a `role="alert"` message, so a refusal is spoken
and not merely painted. **Picking a time moves focus to the details heading**
(`tabIndex={-1}`), because the form appears BELOW the grid and a keyboard user
would otherwise tab through every remaining time button to reach what they just
summoned. Contrast fixed: `placeholder-gray-400` was 2.8:1 and failed at any size
(now gray-500 at 4.8), `(optional)` and hint text moved to gray-600 at 7.5, the
selected day pill's second line moved gray-300 to gray-200 on navy, borders
gray-200 to gray-300. Focus rings are `focus-visible` and **navy, not lime** —
lime on white is 1.8:1, and a focus indicator nobody can see is not one.
**Verified live in the browser**, not asserted: `aria-invalid="true"`,
`aria-describedby="bk-phone-hint bk-phone-err"`, `role="alert"`, red-600 border,
and `document.activeElement` = the H2 "Your details" after a time is picked.

### The reconcile job stops shouting into a log

A `pending_retry` row past `RECONCILE_STUCK_HOURS` now EMAILS Michael. The whole
problem with that is flooding: the job runs hourly, so a row stuck a week is 168
identical emails. **The claim is the fix.** Migration
`20260728120000_booking_stuck_alerts.sql` (applied live) adds one row per
`(booking, Toronto day)` with a primary key on the pair and an insert that does
nothing on conflict; `booking_claim_stuck_alert` returns whether THIS call created
it, so two runs racing send exactly one. The alert carries the client's name and
the appointment time on purpose, because the recovery action is putting the call
in the calendar by hand. The claim comes BEFORE the retry, so a run that dies
halfway leaves it claimed rather than sending twice. A store it cannot reach
returns false and sends nothing, which is the safe direction: the stuck row is
still NAMED in the job log every hour.

**Live posture proven** as the anon role: table SELECT refused `42501`, wrong
operator secret refused `42501`, and the claim returned `true, false, false` on
three calls for the same booking on the same day. The synthetic row
`939c8d2d-ccdf-42ab-90fd-f801ff59e4d5` was LEFT IN PLACE per the append-only rule.

### The copy gate now covers the pages, not just the mail

`tests/booking-email.test.ts` gated every word an email sends. Nothing gated the
words on the PAGES. The first cut of the new sweep read the whole source and was
wrong, loudly: `!` is the negation operator on nearly every line and `;` separates
fields in a TypeScript type, both banned in copy and unavoidable in code. The
extractor now pulls JSX text nodes and copy-bearing attributes, STRIPPING
interpolations from a run rather than rejecting the run for having one (an earlier
cut threw away "Times are shown in your timezone ({tz})." for exactly that). It is
tested against going vacuous, and tested that it would catch a banned character.

### The swap-gate checklist

| Row | Verdict | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Two-timezone render | **PASS (this session, live)** | `2026-07-28T14:00Z` renders 10:00 AM Tue Toronto / 7:00 AM Tue Vancouver / 11:00 AM Tue Halifax. Day-boundary case: a Toronto Tue 4:45 PM slot is Wed 8:45 AM in Auckland and ALL THREE day buckets shift forward, which is the "grouped by the visitor's day, not the host's" rule doing its job. |
| Double-booking race | **PASS (session two)** | Partial unique index on `(agent_id, starts_at)` for live rows; `slot_taken` is the true microsecond race. Not re-run this session (it costs a junk booking whose token lives only in an email). |
| Cancel and rebook | **PASS (session two, live)** | Status cancelled with reason and `cancelled_by`, calendar event deleted, id cleared. |
| Reschedule round trip | **PASS (session two, live)** | Row moved, `original_starts_at` kept, `rescheduled_count` 1, old event removed, new one written, mail sent. |
| Consent row with timestamp | **PASS (both halves)** | Positive half session two, live: CASL stamped `Express` / dated / sourced / verbatim sentence. Negative half THIS session, live: an unticked box left `sms_consent false`, `consented_at null`, and wrote nothing to Zoho. |
| Reminder fires | **BLOCKED, not failed** | The job path is proven live this session (200 with a structured log, `considered 0` because the booking is not yet in the window, wrong secret 401). The CLOCK is the blocker, see below. |
| Calendar event carries the client number | **PASS (this session, live)** | Read back off the real Outlook calendar: location `Phone call to (226) 770-8880`, body `Call them at (226) 770-8880`. |
| Clean inbox render | **PARTIAL** | Confirmation sent and stamped (`confirmation_sent_at` set). The rendered inbox is Michael's to eyeball, and it is in his inbox now. |
| No-slots day view | **PASS (this session, render-proven)** | Both widths. |
| Provider-outage state | **PASS (this session, render-proven)** | Forced by pointing `MS_CALENDAR_UPN` at a mailbox that does not exist in a LOCAL-ONLY gitignored env override, screenshotted at both widths, restored byte-identical. |
| Expired / already-used token pages | **PASS (this session)** | Three distinguished states plus the one deliberately vague not-found, which is render-proven at both widths. |
| Booking-not-found page | **PASS (this session, render-proven)** | Both widths. |
| Accessibility of the /book flow | **PASS (this session, verified live)** | See above. |
| Rate limiting | **PASS (this session)** | Sliding, two-tier, per-IP and per-email, 27 new unit tests including the fixed-window boundary attack. |
| Reconcile alert | **PASS (this session, posture proven live)** | Claim-then-send, once per booking per Toronto day. |

**Both widths** (standing rule, B8a): 1280 and 375 measured on the picker, the
details form with a live validation error, the outage card, the `/book`
not-found, and the manage not-found. Zero horizontal overflow at 375 measured by
`scrollWidth === clientWidth`. Zero console errors.

### The live proof booking, and what lands in Michael's inbox

Made through the real confirm route against the real calendar, the real Zoho org,
and real Resend.

- **Booking `e07588fc-64b3-43d7-99b7-870f8746692b`**, Discovery call,
  **Wednesday 29 July 2026 at 9:00 AM Toronto** (`2026-07-29T13:00:00Z`), 15
  minutes, client Michael Fox, `+12267708880`.
- Calendar event **written** on the first attempt, live on the real calendar,
  carrying the number in both the location and the body.
- **Confirmation email already sent** to `mfox@foxmortgage.ca`. It carries the
  ics and the manage link. **That link is how the booking gets cancelled** — the
  reschedule token is never returned to a browser by design.
- Zoho: **a LEAD was created**, id `7112178000006506006`, because
  `mfox@foxmortgage.ca` is not an existing Contact. **This is junk in the
  production CRM and should be deleted** once the proof is done. No CASL fields
  were written (the box was left unticked, and `createBookingLead` does not write
  them anyway).
- **DEVIATION from the brief, stated:** the brief asked for roughly 26 hours out.
  26 hours from the booking moment is 2:18 AM, which is not a slot. 9:00 AM
  Wednesday is the EARLIEST bookable time that clears the 24-hour reminder-lead
  guard, so it is the closest honest approximation. Its reminder window is
  **2026-07-28 08:00 to 10:00 AM Toronto**, which lands inside Michael's Tuesday.

### The reminder will NOT fire on its own yet, and this is the one blocker

n8n `Uc9CoYm4B2XSpN5m` "Booking — Reminders & Calendar Reconcile (hourly)" is
`active: false` with `triggerCount: 0`, **and its HTTP node has no credential
bound at all** — so activating it as it stands would POST without
`x-bridge-secret` and collect silent 401s.

Two steps, both in the n8n UI, both Michael's:

1. Bind credential **`Fox Bridge Sweep`** (`ju9Qj1NJTOg8P0SB`, the same
   `x-bridge-secret` header auth the underwriting sweep uses) to the **Run
   Booking Jobs** node.
2. Activate the workflow.

**Deliberately not done by this session.** Activating starts an hourly production
job that sends client mail on a schedule, which is an outward-facing standing
change the brief did not authorize, and session two named it as Michael's step.
Done before 8:00 AM Tuesday, the reminder fires naturally in the 8-10 AM window
and the observation completes. After that, the reconcile arm still drains and the
reminder can be fired by hand with one POST.

### Deferred, with reasons

- **Booking's own cron secret.** Needs an env var. Boundary. Session four.
- **The duplicate-event residual** session two logged (create succeeds at Graph,
  response lost, reconcile creates a duplicate). The mechanism is identified —
  Microsoft Graph's `transactionId` on the event create is exactly the
  idempotency key session two asked for — but wiring it safely needs a live Graph
  experiment on real calendar events, which is outside this brief's seven scope
  items. Session four, with the mechanism named so it starts from a known place.
- **A durable rate limiter.** Decided against, reasoning above and in the file.

`20260728120000_booking_stuck_alerts.sql`. Rewritten:

## 2026-07-28 — Native booking engine, session four of four

The admin surface and the cutover checklist. Base `3ca9584`, **1153 tests**
(from 1112, +41), tsc clean, `next build` green, one migration applied live. No
env vars, no vercel commands, no Azure changes, no new vendors, **no swap
performed**, `/book` still out of public navigation.

### The Availability page

`/portal/admin/availability`, in the Command Centre shell, behind a new
`booking.manage` authority key (admin only). Four tabs, each saving on its own
so a bad Tuesday never blocks a good meeting-type change.

- **Weekly hours.** All seven days, several windows per day, times in the host
  timezone. A day with no windows is closed, and the store DELETES the row
  rather than writing an empty one, so a closed day has exactly one
  representation in the database.
- **Closed days.** Date overrides, closed all day or open with different hours.
  An open override REFUSES to save with no windows, because that silently means
  closed and is not what the person asked for. Removing an override returns the
  day to the weekly hours rather than closing it.
- **Meeting types.** Every setting with its bounds printed under the field, an
  active toggle, the public URL shown, and intake questions listed read-only.
  EDIT ONLY, deliberately: creating a type mints a public URL, which can wait
  for a real second agent. The slug is not editable, because it IS the URL and
  changing it breaks every link already sent.
- **Upcoming.** Confirmed bookings with the client's number, the calendar state,
  and a two-step cancel.

**PER-AGENT BY DESIGN, Michael-only in practice.** Every store function takes an
agent id; the id is resolved SERVER-SIDE from a config slug
(`BOOKING_HOST_SLUG`) through `booking_agent_for_slug`; and **no route accepts an
agent id from the browser**, which is asserted by a test, because a
client-supplied agent id on an admin write is exactly how one host would edit
another host's calendar.

### The cancel reuses the client's own path, and that is the point

`bookingForAdmin(id)` returns the IDENTICAL `TokenBooking` shape the token
lookup returns (both map through one `mapTokenBooking`), so an admin cancel is
`cancelBooking({ by: 'admin' })` and nothing else. The client's cancellation
email, the calendar removal, and the Zoho note all live inside that function. An
admin cancel that took a shortcut would be a booking the client never heard was
cancelled, still sitting in their calendar. A test asserts the route calls
`cancelBooking` and never `cancelBookingRow`.

Deliberately NOT time-gated: `SELF_SERVE_CUTOFF_HOURS` exists because a client
changing a call with an hour's notice needs a person. Michael IS that person.

### A loud validator, because the read path's is deliberately quiet

`normalizeWindows` DROPS anything malformed or inverted. On the way out of the
database that is correct, since a bad row must never widen availability. On the
way IN it is a trap: type nine to eight, get a green save, and believe Tuesday
is open when it is closed. `lib/booking/admin.ts` is the write-path twin and it
is loud, refusing and naming the problem. It also REFUSES overlaps rather than
merging them, because a merged day is a day the person never described. Both
behaviours are tested against `normalizeWindows` side by side so the difference
stays deliberate.

**The bounds are tested against the DATABASE.** `EVENT_TYPE_BOUNDS` is parsed
against the column checks in migration 20260727160000 by a test that reads the
SQL. If they ever drift, the page accepts something Postgres refuses and the
failure reaches Michael as a 502 instead of a helpful sentence.

### The copy gate became a module

Three sessions restated the same five rules in prose and re-encoded them in
tests. `lib/booking/copy-gate.ts` is now the single source, because session four
gave Michael a TEXT BOX: a meeting type's name and description are typed on this
page and rendered on the PUBLIC booking page, so they are client copy written by
someone not thinking about a copy gate. The editor warns inline with the same
rules the tests sweep with, and warns rather than refuses, because it is his
desk.

### Verified

- **tsc clean, `next build` green, 1153 tests** (+41: windows validation against
  normalizeWindows, override rules, every event-type bound at both ends, the
  cross-field increment rule, intake questions, the bounds-match-the-database
  sweep, the copy-gate module, and eight wiring contracts).
- **LIVE POSTURE, proven as the anon role:** all eight new functions refuse
  without the operator secret (42501); `booking_hours`, `booking_overrides`, and
  `booking_event_types` still refuse a direct SELECT (42501); the read path with
  the secret returns host `mike`/America/Toronto, 5 weekday rows, 0 overrides, 4
  meeting types, and session three's proof booking in `upcoming`.
- **RENDER PROOF** (blessed pattern: local dev on the dev Clerk instance,
  ephemeral TEST admin created and DELETED in-session, confirmed gone with a
  404): all four tabs at 1280, zero horizontal overflow at 375 measured on every
  tab, zero console errors.
- **LIVE WRITE ROUND TRIP through the real UI:** a disposable
  `2099-12-25` closed day saved ("2099-12-25 saved."), appeared in the list,
  then removed ("...follows the usual weekly hours again"); an inverted Monday
  window was REFUSED in the UI with "Window 1: the end time must be after the
  start time." and did not save. The database was re-read afterwards and is
  byte-for-byte as it started: 0 overrides, Monday to Friday 9 to 5.
- **A RENDER PROOF CAUGHT A REAL BUG.** Every time field rendered the full width
  of its card: `${INPUT} w-32` puts two width utilities on one element, and
  which wins is decided by their order in the generated stylesheet rather than
  in the class attribute. `w-full` won. Fixed with a separate `TIME_INPUT`
  constant and the reason written above it.

### The cutover inventory

`docs/booking-cutover-inventory-2026-07-28.md`, and it corrected two premises:

1. **The swap is a CONSTANT, not an env var.** `CONTACT.bookingUrl` in
   `lib/contact.ts` is a hardcoded literal read by eight portal surfaces. There
   is no `BOOKING_URL` to flip. A SECOND change is easy to miss: four Support
   pages carry the literal subtitle "Schedule time via Zoho Bookings", which is
   client-visible copy naming a vendor that would no longer be involved.
2. **`RENEWAL_CALENDAR_URL` is in fox-underwriting and has NEVER been set.**
   Touches needing it HOLD rather than send without it, so **the drip has never
   sent a Zoho Bookings link to anybody.** That collapses the retirement
   question.
3. The SMM wizard does not link to Zoho Bookings (its CTA goes to `/contact`),
   so there is no repoint to undo, only an optional one to make.
4. `[CalendarLink]` exists only in `docs/JOURNEY.md`. The real pin is
   `{calendar_link}`. Naming drift in a document.

**Retirement recommendation: 2026-10-27, three months after the swap**, with a
check first: if the Zoho Bookings account took zero bookings in the previous 30
days, close it, otherwise move out a month. The reasoning is the renewal
cadence, whose longest touch reaches a client 90 days out, so three months
covers a full outreach cycle plus the tail of anyone sitting on a hand-pasted
link. The live surfaces need no grace period at all, because they render the
constant rather than a copy of it.

## 2026-07-28 — Michael's signature on the booking emails

One job, done at one source. Base `8d6178a`, **1163 tests** (from 1153, +10),
tsc clean. No engine logic, no env, no Azure, no nav.

### Where it lives

`lib/booking/signature.ts`, NEW, and it is the only place the signature exists.
A shared `signOff()` already sat in `email-copy.ts` feeding all four client
emails, so this was a replace-in-place rather than an extraction. Confirmation,
reminder, reschedule, and cancel all read from it, and changing the signature is
now one file.

**The phone and email are NOT written into it.** They come from `lib/contact.ts`
through `CONTACT`, because `tests/contact-number.test.ts` forbids a phone-shaped
literal anywhere in app, lib, components, or config except that file. Hardcoding
the number into a signature is precisely the drift that guard exists to stop. A
test now asserts the consequence, not just the rule: the signature source
contains `CONTACT.phone.display` and no phone-shaped literal at all.

### Two premises in the brief were wrong, and both mattered

1. **The booking emails were PLAIN TEXT, not HTML.** Every Resend send passed
   `text:` and nothing else. The brief said "the HTML emails". This matters
   because **"Click HERE" cannot be a link in plain text**, and the signature is
   built around that link.
2. So the mail is now **multipart**. `BuiltMail` gained an optional `html`, and
   `finish()` builds both parts from ONE authored body: the text part is the
   body plus the signature's text form, the html part is that same body rendered
   through `bodyToHtml` plus the signature's html form. No sentence is written
   twice, so the two parts cannot drift. The note to Michael stays text-only,
   deliberately, and a test asserts its `html` is undefined.

**The plain-text part spells the application address out** on its own line under
"Start your online application here:", because telling a text-only reader to
click a word that does nothing is worse than giving them the address.

### The one character changed

The supplied signature ended the application sentence with an exclamation point.
The client copy gate bans it. One character became a period, nothing else, and a
test pins the exact sentence.

### The gate, extended

The copy sweep only ever read `mail.text`. It now also strips tags from the html
part and sweeps the visible words, which is the half that could have shipped an
em dash unseen. Note that `brokerName` and `brokerId` in the application URL do
NOT trip the `\bbroker\b` rule (the word is followed by a letter), and they are
query parameters rather than prose in any case.

### Verified

- tsc clean, **1163 tests** (+10: every kind carries every signature line, the
  name and title exactly as supplied, the period on the application sentence,
  HERE resolving to the application URL in html and the address spelled out in
  text, the website link, the phone sourced from CONTACT rather than written,
  body and html agreeing, the manage link clickable, and the host note staying
  plain).
- **RENDER PROOF, all four kinds, both widths.** Built to disk, served from the
  dev server, read back from the DOM: every kind carries MICHAEL FOX / Mortgage
  Agent, Level 2 / License M21000367; HERE resolves to the application URL with
  both query parameters intact; the website line resolves to
  `https://www.foxmortgage.ca`; the email line is a mailto; the manage link is
  clickable on booked, reminder, and reschedule and correctly ABSENT on cancel,
  which has nothing left to manage. Zero exclamation points. Zero horizontal
  overflow at 375 on all four, line structure holding, and the 560px email
  column behaving at 1280.

### A judgment call, flagged

The signature arrived as three markdown lines. Single newlines collapse in
markdown, so "MICHAEL FOX Mortgage Agent, Level 2 License M21000367" is almost
certainly three lines in Michael's real signature rather than one run-on. It is
rendered as separate lines (name, title, licence, then phone, email, website,
then the application sentence), which is the standard shape and reads correctly
at both widths. If he wanted the literal three-line grouping, it is a one-file
change in `signatureTextLines` and `signatureHtml`.

### Files

New: `lib/booking/signature.ts`. Touched: `lib/booking/email-copy.ts` (signOff
now delegates, BuiltMail gained html, four builders route through finish),
`lib/booking/email.ts` (both client sends pass html), `tests/booking-email.test.ts`.

### 2026-07-20 note — P1: BDM contacts on the lender card (the portal surface over W1)
The lender detail page (Rates → By lender → a lender) gains a Contacts front: approved BDM / underwriter contacts render with tap-to-call + tap-to-email, and an admin adds / edits (supersede) / retires a contact through the human gate. Portal surface only over W1's workbench engine (fox-underwriting `0e1cd07`, migrations 0051+0052); NO workbench code this session, no migration, no new env var. 944 → **952 tests** (+8), tsc clean, `next build` green, shell lime audit green. Committed NOT pushed. FINDINGS-FIRST: (1) the by-lender view keys on the QUOTE slug (`rate_quotes.lender_slug`, the `?lender` param); the workbench contact endpoints key by whatever `lender_slug` the human enters and the read groups by it, so create + read both use the card's own slug and reconcile by construction. (2) STANDING QUESTION resolved with a LIVE query — the book has **25** distinct approved slugs, the palette's static `HAND_WRITTEN_LENDER_SLUGS` had 23, the exact gap is `duca` + `meridian` (every other static slug is in the book; no stale entries). The card shape carries a PRE-BUILT `tel` (RFC-3966, `;ext=`) + `phone_display` — rendered, never re-derived. A create AUTO-APPROVES (no pending state); an edit SUPERSEDES (the response is the NEW row id, so the card refetches); a retire needs a reason; a 409 is a DUPLICATE, so `surface409` passes the workbench's real reason through instead of "Already decided." Legacy prose contact rows are skipped by the endpoint (returns `[]`), so a lender with only prose reads as the teaching empty state. (4) DEVIATION from "Contacts on the grid card front": the grid card is a single `<button>`, so interactive tel:/mailto: cannot nest without restructuring a core surface — the interactive section mounts on the DETAIL page (`LenderPage`, a `<div>`); §1's "grid loads from the bulk read in ONE call" is honored by ONE bulk read at the `RatesLenders` top feeding both a non-interactive "N contacts on file" line per grid card AND the detail section (no per-card fetch). VERIFIED: 952 tests (`tests/lender-contacts.test.ts` +8; demo +1 canned-read/writes-blocked; authority admin-only key). PROVEN LIVE (dev-Clerk ephemeral TEST admin created + DELETED + confirmed gone): DEMO — MCAP card renders Jordan Wells (`tel:+16475550142;ext=218`, display "+1 (647) 555-0142 x218", + mailto) + Priya (email-only), rfa shows the teaching empty state, no write controls; both demo write routes → 403 "Demo mode is read-only."; 1280 + 375 measured ZERO horizontal overflow (contacts span 359 < 375). ### 2026-07-20 note — The Scenario deal bar + teaching results (per-lender "why excluded", honest by shape)
The Scenario tab's vertical input rail becomes a compact horizontal DEAL BAR across the top, the ranked matches take the FULL WIDTH below as the hero, and a new collapsed "Excluded (N)" section names each left-out lender with ONE plain-words reason. Presentation + a pure teaching READ; NO matching or ranking change, no workbench/classifier change. 926 → **938 tests** (+12), tsc clean, `next build` green, shell + deals-surface lime audits green. Committed NOT pushed. FINDINGS-FIRST: the inputs are `ScenarioRail`'s selects (purpose/occupancy/class/term/rate type/cash back/amortization) + `CommittedNumberField` (amount/value, already blur/Enter-committed) + checkbox groups (borrower profiles, commitments) + show-restricted; PROVINCE is NOT a rail input (defaults ON, prefill only). Client-side exclusion data today: `structuralMatch` drops a quote on productClass/term/rate type/cashback/LTV band/rental — but returns `null` (silently absent, NO reason); `scenarioExclusions` buckets only the ELIGIBILITY exclusions (province_ineligible/channel_unavailable/transaction_mismatch/program_restricted/province_unknown) on structurally-matching quotes; an `ExclusionNotes` component already named excluded lenders but PER-REASON bucket. LOUD DEVIATIONS: (1) the brief's "Wrong lender tier" example is NOT a scenario exclusion — tier is a savings-engine concept, never a reason here; (2) "Does not lend on uninsurable files" is a structural class mismatch that returns null today with no reason — surfacing per-lender structural reasons needs a new pure READ over the loaded book (reuses the existing filter order + verdicts, changes no matching/ranking, touches neither the workbench nor the eligibility classifier — within the hard boundary); the honest copy is "no X rate on file", never the brief's overstated "does not lend on X". THE CHANGE: `DealBar` (replaces `ScenarioRail`) reflows the SAME inputs/handlers/testids into a responsive grid (2/3/5 cols) + a "More filters" disclosure for the qualifier groups; the two-column `[290px 1fr]` grid is gone (results full-width). NEW pure `lib/scenario.ts` `quoteBlockReason(q,s)` (the first filter one quote fails, in structuralMatch's order then eligibility) + `lenderExclusions(quotes,s)` (per lender with real quotes but none in the ranking, its FURTHEST-progressing quote's block = the decisive reason; province_unknown stays IN flagged, never excluded), both unit-tested. `ExcludedLenders` + `exclusionReason` render the collapsed per-lender view; the old per-reason `ExclusionNotes` + the tier-unconfirmed footnote are gone. ADVERSARIAL REVIEW (3 dims × refuting verify; 6 raised / **5 CONFIRMED, all fixed + tested** / 1 refuted): all honesty-copy (matching/ranking confirmed untouched) — (MED) the 'restricted' reason was FALSE for undisclosed/unclassified restrictions (eligibility_unknown; NO profile/commitment unlocks them) → split into a distinct `restricted_undisclosed` kind driven by the verdict's `undisclosedRestriction` flag ("The closest rate carries a restriction the rate sheet does not name. Confirm with the lender."); (MED) 'transaction' overstated a per-quote restriction as lender-wide policy → closest-rate form; (LOW) 'occupancy'/'cashback none' asserted a whole-book universal ("Only rental priced rates on file") → closest-rate form, with term/rate_type/ltv/amortization reframed the same. Reason copy is now honest BY SHAPE: lender-wide truths (class, province) as whole-book statements, everything else as "the closest rate …" (describes the furthest quote, always true). VERIFIED: 938 tests (`tests/scenario.test.ts` +12). PROVEN LIVE (blessed pattern, dev-Clerk ephemeral TEST admin created + DELETED): ON conventional 5yr fixed $650K/$850K → the horizontal deal bar, 14 ranked matches as the hero, "Excluded (11)" each reason verified TRUE against the book (9× "No conventional rate on file", Kootenay "Not available in Ontario. Licensed in BC.", Scotiabank named-restricted), 14+11=25 lenders (reconciles); typing = ZERO navigations, blur commits once (input-commit intact, book cache unchanged from the consolidation session); demo empty book reads as a TEACHING state ("No approved quotes match this scenario") not broken; both widths 1280 + 375 zero horizontal overflow. ### 2026-07-20 note — Ask Fox truncation fix + honest errors (the production "Unterminated string at position 6000" crash)
FINDINGS-FIRST, LOUD DEVIATION: the logged failure `[agent] turn failed: Unterminated string in JSON at position 6000` is NOT a model max_tokens/stop_reason truncation (the brief's framing). The loop (`lib/agent/loop.ts`) streams, checks stop_reason (refusal/max_tokens/tool_use already handled), and NEVER JSON.parses the model reply — the SDK does. The ONLY JSON.parse that throws at position 6000 is a TOOL: `lib/agent/tools.ts` `knowledge_lookup` capped the lender profile with `JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(d.profile, null, 0).slice(0,6000))` — `.slice(0,6000)` cuts JSON mid-token, `JSON.parse` throws for any profile whose compact JSON >6000 chars, the handler has NO try/catch, so the throw propagated to the loop catch, which logged the failure AND emitted the misleading "could not reach the model" (the model was reached fine; a tool crashed). AGENT_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS=16000 is generous, NOT tight (not raised). THE FIX: (1) `cappedProfile` (NEW pure helper in tools.ts, exported) caps a value's size WITHOUT slice-then-reparse — a small profile passes through as the structured object; an oversized one becomes a valid `{truncated,note,text}` object carrying the first N chars as text; never throws; `knowledge_lookup` uses it. (2) the loop wraps the tool dispatch so a THROWN tool becomes a failed `tool_result` (is_error) the model responds to and the turn CONTINUES — no single tool bug crashes a turn again. (3) the catch differentiates THREE honest cases, plain words, NO semicolons, every one saying "Nothing was written": could-not-reach (network/API/auth/rate-limit), and a NEW could-not-read branch (`isUnreadableReplyError` — a JSON/SyntaxError, distinct from could-not-reach); the answer-was-cut-off is the existing max_tokens branch. (4) on ANY caught error the loop returns `text:''`, so a failed turn never persists a partial as the reply (never-partially-written). INTERPRETATION stated: never-partially-written applies to UNREADABLE/failed replies; a genuine max_tokens cutoff is VALID text kept with an honest cut-off note (the brief's own "fail with honest copy about the answer being cut off"), not discarded. VERIFIED: 921 → **926 tests** (+5: cappedProfile reproduces the exact "Unterminated string" throw + proves the fix round-trips; the loop honest-read test throws the exact production SyntaxError shape → `error` contains "could not read" not "could not reach", `text===''`, "Nothing was written", no semicolon; a tool-throw test proves the turn survives; isUnreadableReplyError classification), tsc clean, `next build` green, shell lime audit green. DEMO render (dev-Clerk ephemeral TEST admin created + DELETED): Ask Fox in demo answered a long question with the canned `[Demo]` reply (route short-circuits before any model/workbench/Zoho call — zero real reads), no error copy, zero overflow, the "not configured yet: add ANTHROPIC_API_KEY" banner visible. DEFERRED LOUD: the LIVE model turn can't run locally (no ANTHROPIC_API_KEY by design + the build guardrail forbids setting it; the dev Clerk instance can't mint the `gates` token knowledge_lookup needs) — proven by the unit tests + demo render, the live model turn is Michael's step (the Agent-session precedent). Committed NOT pushed. See docs/ask-fox-truncation-2026-07-20.md + the 2026-07-20 ledger entry.

### 2026-07-20 note — Lenders one-row consolidation (the two tab rows become one; the book fetch decouples from searchParams)
The Lenders page's two stacked tab rows collapse into ONE row — Scenario, Rates, Promos, Intel, Knowledge — and `getRateQuotesFull` gets a short cache so scenario/select changes stop re-reading the ~1,257-row book (the input-commit follow-up). Presentation + routing + one cache; NO data or pricing logic changed. 914 → **921 tests** (+7), tsc clean, `next build` green, shell lime audit green. Committed NOT pushed. FINDINGS: the old structure was TWO layers over the same `tab` param — outer `rates|intel|knowledge` (page) + inner `scenario|lenders|promos|all` (`RatesTabs`, client), disjoint values composing. THE COLLISION, resolved: `?tab=rates` was NEVER durable (the inner shell's useEffect always client-rewrote a non-inner tab to `?tab=scenario`), so `rates` is a FREE value for the new merged tab; only three producers of `?tab=rates` existed (the next.config redirect, a changelog link, the page's own TabBar), all repointed. THE CHANGE: `lib/lenders-tabs.ts` (NEW pure: `LENDERS_TAB_KEYS`, `resolveLendersTab`, `lendersTabPermission`, `isRateEngineTab`, unit-tested) drives ONE `TabBar`, per-tab permission (scenario/rates/promos → rates.view; intel → intel.view; knowledge → knowledge.view). The old inner "Lenders" + "All quotes" become the Rates tab's two views (`?view=lenders|all`) behind a By-lender/All-quotes toggle in NEW `components/admin/RatesBook.tsx` (client; absorbed the All-quotes prime-reference fetch from the deleted `RatesTabs.tsx`) — same rows grouped or flat, no data/pricing change, no name collision (the page is Lenders; the inner control is "By lender"). `components/admin/lenders/RatesTab.tsx` → renamed `RatesEngine.tsx`, renders the active rate tab with per-tab fetching (promos needs no book). REDIRECTS (nothing 404s): next.config `/portal/admin/rates` → `?tab=scenario`; in-page `?tab=lenders → ?tab=rates&view=lenders` and `?tab=all → ?tab=rates&view=all` (preserving every other param); relinked the deal-room find-rates prefill (`/portal/admin/rates?… → /portal/admin/lenders?tab=scenario&…`) and RatesPromos "Open lender" (`tab=lenders → tab=rates`). FETCH DECOUPLING: `getRateQuotesFull` gains a 2-minute agent-keyed in-process cache (house norm — slimDealsCache; covers a page visit AND the 60s bell poll; failures never cached), so every param-driven re-render is a cache hit; matching stays client-side over the loaded `quotes` prop; the old inner-tab session memory is dropped (default Scenario, which also removes an on-load navigation). DEVIATIONS (loud): (1) DEMO — `getRateQuotesFull` scopes to `DEMO_AGENT_ID` (no rows), so the book renders EMPTY in demo (no real lender data on a demo screen); pending-sheets/intel/claims are demo-guarded fixtures; the All-quotes prime reference stays real per the Session 9 contract; one real Supabase read still fires (scoped to the non-existent demo agent) — existing pre-change behavior. (2) session tab memory dropped. Saved scenarios store scenario DIMENSIONS only (not tab), and `scenario` is unchanged, so their deep links resolve. VERIFIED: 921 tests (`tests/lenders-tabs.test.ts` 6, `tests/rate-quotes-cache.test.ts` 1 — three calls → one DB read; the deals-surface lime audit's hardcoded list updated for the rename + `RatesBook`). PROVEN LIVE (blessed pattern, dev-Clerk ephemeral TEST admin created + DELETED): the single row (Scenario·Rates·Promos·Intel·Knowledge, default Scenario) + the Rates By-lender/All-quotes toggle (`?view` deep-linkable, lender cards + dense table); redirects live (`/rates → ?tab=scenario`; `?tab=lenders&lender=mcap → ?tab=rates&view=lenders&lender=mcap`; `?tab=all → ?tab=rates&view=all`; `/intel → ?tab=intel`); a scenario deep link resolves on Scenario with the amount applied; the NETWORK PROOF via a temporary cache-miss log (removed before commit) on a cold server — initial load 3 book reads (the concurrent page + bell + desk requests on the empty cache), then a scripted sequence of scenario edits + a select change + a tab switch + the view toggle = ZERO additional book reads; both widths 1280 + 375 zero horizontal overflow; demo render clean. Committed NOT pushed. See docs/lenders-consolidation-2026-07-20.md + the 2026-07-20 ledger entry.

### 2026-07-20 note — The Today calendar band goes live (Microsoft Graph, read-only)
The calendar card in Your day stops being a connect state and renders Michael's real day from his Microsoft calendar. Read-only, server-side, fail-soft; NO Graph WRITE exists anywhere in this build. FINDINGS FIRST: greenfield (no prior Microsoft/Graph plumbing anywhere); the repo has no `server-only` npm package (comment convention only), so server-only is the comment + a static TEST; all four env vars present (`MS_TENANT_ID/CLIENT_ID/CLIENT_SECRET/CALENDAR_UPN`) — two carried a stray leading space, so `msEnv()` `.trim()`s every value; a live probe confirmed the creds work AND locked the Graph shape: with `Prefer: outlook.timezone="America/Toronto"`, `calendarView` returns start/end as NAIVE Toronto wall-clock strings. THE INTEGRATION (`lib/ms-calendar.ts`, server-only): client-credentials token (`.../oauth2/v2.0/token`, scope `.default`) cached in-process to 60s-before-expiry, never logged; `GET /users/{UPN}/calendarView` for the exact Toronto day, `$orderby=start/dateTime`, `$top=50`, slim shape (subject, naive start/end, isAllDay, location displayName, online HINT — no attendees, no bodies, no join URLs); a 60s in-process lites cache is the "short revalidate" (only the network read is cached; statuses re-derive from a fresh clock each render; failures never cached). THE BAND (`components/admin/today/YourDay.tsx`): four states from a `CalendarResult` (mirrors `UwResult`) — connect (absent env) / "Calendar is not available right now." (runtime failure) / teaching empty / the time-ordered list (muted past, ink-navy accent + "now" marker on the in-progress event, all-day neutral); NO lime (the shell audit walks this file). FAIL-SOFT, load-bearing: `getTodayCalendar` NEVER throws to the page (missing env → `{configured:false}`; any Graph/token failure caught → `{configured:true, ok:false}`), so it is `Promise.all`-safe and a Graph outage degrades only that card. SECURITY: secrets/UPN in env only, read ONLY in `lib/ms-calendar.ts`, never NEXT_PUBLIC, never logged (status codes only) or in a URL query; imported only by the server page + TYPE-ONLY by demo-fixtures (no cycle, no client bundle); `tests/ms-calendar.test.ts` ASSERTS this. HARDENING NOTE for Michael (in the report): the app permission is tenant-wide `Calendars.Read`; an ApplicationAccessPolicy can scope it to his mailbox alone. DEMO: `demoCalendarLites` (obviously fictional, all four states) through the SAME pure mapper, zero real reads (asserted). ADVERSARIAL REVIEW (4 dims × refuting verify; 5 raised / **2 CONFIRMED, both fixed** + regressions / 3 refuted): (HIGH) the calendarView window was offset-less (`todayYMD T00:00:00`) — Graph reads that as UTC and the Prefer header only sets RESPONSE rendering, so the window was the UTC day shifted 4-5h (today's evening events dropped, yesterday's late ones leaked as stale rows; a daytime test can't reveal it — which is why the live probe passed); FIXED to send UTC instants of the Toronto day (`torontoDayStartISO`, DST-correct), verified live. (MED) `localMinutes` discarded the date so cross-midnight/multi-day timed events got wrong status/sort; FIXED with `localOffsetMinutes` (date-aware minutes-relative-to-today) for status+sort while labels keep the event's own wall clock, + regression tests. VERIFIED: tsc clean, `next build` green, 897 → **914 tests** (+17: ms-calendar 16, demo +1), shell lime audit green. PROVEN LIVE (blessed pattern, dev-Clerk ephemeral TEST admin created + DELETED in-session, read-only): real event ("11:00 AM · Recurring Mortgage Meeting", correct Toronto time/order, zero console errors) at 1280 + 375; demo multi-state (all-day/past/now/upcoming) at 1280 + 375; BOTH failure paths end-to-end (env blanked → connect state; bad secret → the quiet line + server log `[ms-calendar] token HTTP 401`), page intact in both, `.env.local` restored clean; zero horizontal overflow at every width. Committed NOT pushed. See docs/ms-calendar-2026-07-20.md + the 2026-07-20 ledger entry.

### 2026-07-20 note — Task two-way: complete a Zoho task from Today (the portal's FIRST Zoho write)
The Tasks card on Today gains a checkbox that completes the task in Zoho. Zoho stays the source of truth; the portal is a remote control, never a second ledger — no local task state can drift (a completed task simply drops from the next `getTasksDue` read, which already excludes Completed). FINDINGS FIRST: (1) write capability CONFIRMED with a disposable round trip (create → read → update→Completed → reopen → DELETE, all success, nothing left behind, real tasks untouched) — the credential is NOT read-only, no scope change, the brief's read-only contingency does not apply; auth is the existing `getZohoToken()` (`ZOHO_CLIENT_ID/SECRET/REFRESH_TOKEN` at accounts.zoho.com). (2) `getTasksDue` already filters `Status !== 'Completed'`. (3) a `completeZohoTask` already existed (zero app callers), generalized to `setZohoTaskStatus` (+ the non-success check) + a new `getZohoTask` (read prior status). (4) "one server action" is the house gated POST route (`apiPermission` → demo-refuse → validate → act → audit) — a verified Clerk admin session is the SOLE way in; NO machine path (no bridge secret, no service identity). THE PATH: `POST /api/portal/admin/tasks/[id]/status` `{action:'complete'|'reopen'}`, admin-only key `tasks.complete`. complete = read current status → record as prior → set Completed (idempotent). reopen = restore the status the most recent complete recorded (`reopenTargetFrom`, pure; default `Not Started`). Both write a FOXCA `task_action_events` audit row (actor email, task, action, prev/new status, result, timestamp). Loud failure: a Zoho write throw → the failed attempt is audited + 502 "The write did not land"; the checkbox reverts; a landed Zoho write is never reported failed over a best-effort audit hiccup. THE CARD: `components/admin/today/TaskList.tsx` (`'use client'`) — a real `<button role="checkbox">` (Space/Enter complete it, seeding the keyboard pattern), optimistic tick + pending, ~10s undo → reopen, `router.refresh()` after the window drops the completed row, honest revert + one-sentence reason on failure. No lime. STORAGE: migration `20260720120000_task_action_events.sql` (table + 3 `plpgsql security definer` functions requiring `p_operator_secret`, RLS on, grants revoked — the `renewal_events` pattern), APPLIED LIVE to `skfeivzhqvrefnkqjwtj`; `lib/task-events-store.ts` is the FOXCA twin (threads `foxcaOperatorSecret()`); nothing deletes. No new env var (uses the already-set `FOXCA_OPERATOR_SECRET`). 892 → 897 tests, tsc clean, `next build` green, shell lime test green. ### 2026-07-20 note — Today v1: the morning operating page
Home (`app/portal/admin/page.tsx`) rebuilt from an exception-first card pile into the page Michael works out of every morning: it answers what needs me → what is moving → what is at risk, each story told ONCE. Band order (single column, shell untouched): (a) Fraunces greeting + one pulse line (date · funded YTD w/ count · pace delta tinted · open file count); (b) Your day — a teaching calendar empty state (one sentence + a "Connect your Microsoft calendar" action, no fake events, MS integration out of scope) beside the LIVE Zoho task list; (c) Waiting on you as ONE region — the navy DeskStrip + the three decision cards (the only decision-token surface on the page) + ONE danger-tinted exceptions block that LEADS with a file closing this week that still has an overdue condition, then overdue-conditions / flags summary / credentials / stalled sync; a HEALTHY sync flips to a quiet green line at the bottom; (d) Renewal nurture (slim, renewals.view); (e) What's moving — the single lifecycle table (the duplicate pipeline-by-stage census DELETED, its groom line moved to The year); (f) Closings widened 7→30 days, each with a conditions-derived readiness chip; (g) The year — four pacing tiles + the bar + the leak line (lapsed $ / action-window $ / act-now benefit → Beyond funding) + the groom line. Every count still derives from its owning page's loader (the Desk pattern) — the page reshapes, never re-derives. PURE MODEL in `lib/today.ts` (parseDealRef, prioritizeTasks, closingReadiness/buildClosingRows, renewalNurtureBuckets, buildExceptions), unit-tested. Bands in `components/admin/today/*` (none use lime; decision cards stay inline in page.tsx per the enumerated allow-list). NEW `config/targets.ts CLOSINGS_BLOCK_DAYS=30`. Rates summary card DROPPED from Today (pending sheet reviews already surface in Waiting-on-you; the browser lives on Lenders).
DEVIATIONS (findings-first): (1) `getTasksDue` reads NO deal field (no What_Id), so a task's deal link is a file-ref PARSED from the subject and matched to a workbench deal — no new Zoho read; the priority "sitting in Waiting-on-you" arm is NOT derivable (tasks carry no queue linkage), so closing-within-30 is the implemented urgency arm. (2) Tasks-card checkboxes OMITTED (a disabled row control promises an out-of-scope write build and crowds the card; the footer catch-up sweep + Zoho links carry the backlog; NO Zoho writes). (3) Renewal nurture shows only entered/drafts-minted/sent — `replied` + `no-reply-after-full-sequence` need a workbench field that does not exist; while dark (`sentCount` all 0) the band reads "Sends not yet live." (4) The ONE exceptions block ABSORBS (does not shrink) the old rail: imminent-closing-with-overdue top line, overdue conditions, due-soon conditions, flags summary, missing-maturity funded deals, credentials, stalled sync — each gated so it only shows for a role that can act on it (credentials compliance.view, missing-maturity renewals.view, stalled sync status.view so the Status link is never a dead link). (5) A closing with no workbench file — OR a bridged closing whose condition read failed — reads NEUTRAL ("not linked to the workbench yet" / "condition state unavailable"), never a false green. 865 → 892 tests, tsc clean, `next build` green, shell lime test green. ADVERSARIAL REVIEW run (4 skeptics; 10 raised / 9 fixed + regressions / 1 accepted loud-safe). Render-proven at 1280 AND 375 (demo mode, ephemeral dev-Clerk TEST admin created + DELETED, zero real reads, zero horizontal overflow, clean-tab load = zero console errors). Committed NOT pushed. See docs/today-v1-2026-07-20.md + the 2026-07-20 ledger entry.
STANDING RULES added (cross-cutting, inherited by future briefs): (1) RELATIVE DATES — use `lib/dates.ts relativeDay(targetYMD, todayYMD, soonDays=7)` → `{days, tone: danger|caution|neutral|success, label, dueLabel}` for any relative-date + urgency-tinted display; NEVER the decision (lime) token for date urgency; the older per-surface day-diff helpers (compliance `daysUntil`, deals-surface inline) stay but new surfaces use this one. (2) FILE-REF LINKS — every file ref rendered on an admin surface links to its deal room (`parseDealRef` + the workbench-deal join). (3) TEACHING EMPTY STATES — an empty band explains what it is for and the first action in ONE sentence under the client copy rules (no em dashes, no exclamation points, no semicolons, Grade 6, sentence case, verbs on buttons); a not-yet-built integration shows the teaching state + its future action, never fake data.

### 2026-07-18 patch — scenario inputs commit on blur/Enter (no search per keystroke)
Fixed the admin Rates scenario view firing the match engine per digit: the two `type="number"` inputs (Mortgage amount + Property value in `RatesScenario.tsx`) called `setScenario` → `router.replace` on every `onChange`, so typing `1500000` wrote `amount=1`, `amount=15`, … — seven navigations, each a force-dynamic RSC refetch of `getRateQuotesFull` (~1257 rows, ×2) + a full-book client recompute. NOW they commit on blur/Enter via NEW `components/admin/CommittedNumberField.tsx` (local text while typing, commits the parsed value once, resyncs to an external `value` change) over NEW pure `lib/input-commit.ts commitNumericInput` (blur/Enter parser, unit-tested); each `onCommit` is guarded so an unchanged blur never re-searches. FINDINGS: the ONLY per-keystroke defect was these two inputs — the B9 client qualification explorer does NOT share it (pure O(1) in-render `computeQualification`, no network; live slider feedback is the intended UX, unchanged), cmd-K is already debounced (180ms + AbortController), Ask Fox submits on Enter, the RatesBrowser/RatesLenders/Partners filters are client-side `useMemo` over loaded props; the RatesScenario selects/checkboxes navigate once per selection (not a keystroke). LOAD-BEARING WRONG-ASSUMPTION IN THE BRIEF, stated: the server refetch does NOT depend on the scenario params (`getRateQuotesFull` is agent-scoped) — each keystroke re-read the identical book, and the matching is entirely client-side; the same wasteful refetch on every select change is a pre-existing inefficiency, noted as a follow-up (decouple the quotes fetch from searchParams). STANDING RULE added to CLAUDE.md: "No keystroke ever triggers a network call or heavy recompute. Inputs commit on blur or Enter, or debounce at 600ms or more with in-flight cancellation" (scoped to matching/fetching inputs; not the pure-local live recompute). PROOF (live, dev-Clerk ephemeral TEST admin `commit-proof+clerk_test@example.com` created + DELETED, signed in via Clerk JS + dev code 424242): on the real Rates view, typing `1500000` digit-by-digit produced ZERO navigations (`location.search` unchanged, no RSC request with a partial `amount=`); blur fired exactly ONE RSC commit `…&amount=1500000&_rsc=…` and the summary updated to `$1.5M`; Next aborts superseded RSC fetches so a stale result never lands. 860 → **865 tests** (+5), tsc clean, `next build` green, shell lime test green. No migration, no fox-underwriting/Zoho changes. Committed NOT pushed. See docs/input-commit-2026-07-20.md.

### 2026-07-18 patch — qualification stretch bands drive on TDS (not GDS)
Config + test only, one edit to `config/qualification.ts bandKeyForRatios`. B9 first shipped the stretch bands (options/alternatives/conversation) triggering on GDS (the generous reading, finding 3 in the B9 note below). Michael decided they should drive on **TDS**: it carries the client's whole obligation picture (consumer debt included) and is the ratio that actually gates a deal, so the band a client sees reflects their full situation. GREEN "fits" still requires BOTH ratios inside standard limits (GDS ≤ 39 AND TDS ≤ 44); only the amber + navy stretch bands moved from GDS-driven to TDS-driven (`tds ≤ 48` → options, `≤ 60` → alternatives, beyond → conversation). Clean single swap (TDS ≥ GDS always, so the cascade is well-defined). The never-says-no law + band copy are UNCHANGED — accuracy, not warmth. `tests/qualification.test.ts` boundary block rewritten to assert TDS drives the stretch bands (a low-GDS/high-TDS file now lands honestly, the case the GDS reading got wrong) + the reaches-all-four-bands and demo control sets retuned to TDS-safe margins. 859 → **860 tests**, tsc clean, `next build` green. Render proof BOTH widths: a GDS 44.7 / TDS 49.7 file shows *alternatives* (was *options* under GDS), zero horizontal overflow. Committed NOT pushed. See docs/qualification-b9-2026-07-18.md (patch note).

### 2026-07-18 note — B9: the qualification explorer (a form that never says no)
THE FIRST INTERACTIVE client surface: a "Can I afford it?" section on `/portal/file/[token]` that renders ONLY when Michael has published a baseline. The client moves FOUR controls (home price + down payment as slider+field each, monthly property taxes, monthly condo fees); a LOCKED panel shows Michael's file truth (yearly income, monthly debts, heating estimate, contract rate, the derived stress-test rate, amortization) with a quiet reset; the live result is GDS ("Home costs") + TDS ("All your costs") bars with band-boundary ticks and ONE band card. THE LAW, enforced not just documented: this surface NEVER tells a person no — the four bands are `fits` (green, GDS≤39 & TDS≤44) / `options` (amber, either ratio ≤48) / `alternatives` (amber, either ≤60) / `conversation` (navy, beyond 60), every one an invitation with the booking link, because the practice reaches alternative/private/equity+net-worth lenders no ratio form sees. `config/qualification.ts` holds the band boundaries + copy + a `QUALIFICATION_NEVER_SAYS_NO` list; `tests/qualification.test.ts` sweeps the band copy for decline words + copy rules, and `tests/client-portal.test.ts` adds `QualificationExplorer.tsx` to `CLIENT_FACING_SOURCES` + a decline-word + em-dash sweep over the component sources (the review-driven gap-close). REUSE, NEVER RE-DERIVE (the load-bearing decision): `lib/qualification.ts computeQualification` folds the CMHC premium exactly as `purchase-engine.computePurchase` (`cmhcPremiumRate` + capitalization) then calls `affordability-engine.debtService` (the ONE GDS/TDS engine behind the three public tools) — golden-tested to the cent (500000@5.00/25yr → 2908.02, 650000@3.75/30yr → 2999.58 through the qualification path); pure (no node:crypto), so the browser explorer, the admin preview, and node tests run identical code. FINDINGS (findings-first): (1) the brief's minimum-down rule ("10% 500k-1M, 20% over 1M") is STALE — the repo + current law use 5%/10%-to-1.5M/20%, insured cap $1.5M; B9 uses the repo's convention (stated deviation) so the explorer never disagrees with the shipped purchase calculators. (2) No heating estimator exists (only a flat $100 UI default) — heat is a Michael-set locked value, proposal defaults it to $100. (3) "Either ratio ≤ X" binds on GDS since TDS≥GDS always — the deliberately generous never-says-no reading, tested at every named edge, flagged for Michael's co-design. INSURED MATH: below 20% down the premium folds in; at/above 20% none; over $1.5M with <20% down AND below 5% down (LTV>95%) there is no insurable path — the bare loan qualifies and the minimum-down helper flags it (never a NaN, never a failure state). ADMIN: `components/admin/QualificationBaselineCard.tsx` (deal-room Section, navy+StatusChip, lime audit UNCHANGED) — Michael reviews a baseline the platform PROPOSES from the file (income summed latest-per-borrower from income_calcs, the Finmo-requested rate, the deal's purchase_price), edits any value, publishes; per-field provenance ('file'/'default'/'edited') is admin-only, never client-facing; a live preview runs the SAME engine. Rides the existing `client.presentation.manage` authority key (a stated decision — no new key; splittable for B10's per-agent scoping). STORAGE mirrors B8b EXACTLY: FOXCA `client_qualification_baselines` (RLS on, no policies, grants revoked), admin writes gated by the shared `foxca_operator_secret_ok` (NO new secret), a token-hash `client_qualification_for_token` read joining `client_links` (revoked/expired filtered) returning ONLY the published baseline; FROZEN snapshots (a later file change never rewrites a panel the client saw); publishing sets exactly ONE published baseline per deal. Migration `20260718200000` APPLIED LIVE to `skfeivzhqvrefnkqjwtj`; anon posture proven (table select 401; write refused without the secret 42501, succeeds with it; token-keyed read works; wrong token returns nothing; admin list gated), TEST rows created + DELETED, tables empty. VERIFIED: tsc clean, `next build` green, 823 → **859 tests** (+36); demo (the demo client file carries a baseline reaching all four bands with zero real reads; every write DemoWriteBlocked; the admin list a fixture with zero fetch). RENDER PROOFS (forged fox_demo cookie, PUBLIC demo client page, zero real reads, no console errors) at BOTH widths: 1280 all four bands (green 38/43, amber options 43/48 insured $566,947, amber alternatives 54/59 insured, navy conversation 68/73) + 375 stacked + the minimum-down helper ("$35,000"), zero horizontal overflow throughout. ADVERSARIAL REVIEW (4 dimensions × refuting verifiers, 8 raised / **2 CONFIRMED LOW, both fixed** + regression tests / 6 refuted): the down-payment field lacked an upper clamp (typed down > price → "117%"/$0 mortgage; now clamped to the price, price floored to its minimum); a literal em dash in the client pct() fallback uncaught by any sweep (now '-', + the two new source sweeps).
