### 2026-07-18 note — FOXCA-wide operator-secret hardening
Ends the "anon key = service account" posture across the WHOLE FOXCA project (`skfeivzhqvrefnkqjwtj`), not just client links (B7-P) and client presentation (B8b). Migration `20260718190000_foxca_operator_secret_hardening.sql`, **APPLIED LIVE**. The FOXCA anon key is shared with public form-intake, so every admin security-definer function that was `grant execute to anon` was a de facto service account: forge compliance/provisioning/renewal/savings/agent/SMM/view-as rows, read the compliance register + view-as logs + staff PII + failed-form PII. **65 functions HARDENED** with a required `p_operator_secret` (refuse 42501 without it) — the client_link/client_presentation pattern EXACTLY, the SAME `FOXCA_OPERATOR_SECRET` (NO new env), old signatures DROPPED (the overload trap), every `language sql` fn rewritten to plpgsql to raise. ONE shared guard `public.foxca_operator_secret_ok(text)` (not anon-callable) carries the sha256; ONE shared store helper `lib/foxca-secret.ts foxcaOperatorSecret()` (throw-if-unset) threads it. Families hardened: form-intake ack/stats/failures, Ask Fox (agent_*), client constraints + pins, compliance (all), notifications (producer + user writes), people (provision/offboard), renewals, saved-scenario writes, savings-analysis log, SMM (all), view-as; PLUS `client_link_event_record` (closes the B7-P audit-forge residual). STAY OPEN (9, stated): the token-keyed client-page flow (`client_link_resolve`/`_touch`, `client_*_for_token` — the token IS the secret), `mark_form_submission` (public form capture), and the user-scoped READS `notifications_list_for_user`/`notification_prefs_get`/`saved_scenarios_list_for_user`. JUDGMENT stated: admin LIST-reads (compliance register, view-as logs, staff PII) are neither user-scoped nor token-keyed, expose business/PII/audit data off the anon key, so they are hardened too; user-scoped WRITES (notification read-state, saved scenarios) too. DEPLOY-ORDERING, LOUD (B7-P pattern at FOXCA-wide scale): dropping the old signatures means the currently-deployed code (which calls without the secret) errors on EVERY hardened function until this session's code deploys — the WHOLE FOXCA admin surface is DOWN (graceful error states, not crashes) between apply and deploy. PUSH + DEPLOY IMMEDIATELY. VERIFIED: tsc clean, build green, 823 tests (+5: `tests/foxca-secret-hardening.test.ts` — a source-coverage sweep that every hardened store call threads the secret + every exempt read does not, and a runtime pass that each family sends the env value + a missing secret fails loud before any network call). LIVE POSTURE proven as the anon role (python+PostgREST): every hardened function refuses without the secret (42501), succeeds with it, old signature gone (42883). Committed NOT pushed. See docs/foxca-hardening-2026-07-18.md.

### 2026-07-18 note — B8b: the presentation layer (scenarios, graded offers, the pre-approval letter)
The client status page grows from STATUS into VALUE: three surfaces Michael composes in the deal room and PUBLISHES to a client's private link — nothing appears by default. Base `dfbf6c8` (Task 0), **791 → 818 tests**, tsc clean, `next build` green, no fox-underwriting changes, no Zoho writes, no sends. TASK 0 (own commit `dfbf6c8`) — the real closing-date inconsistency: the client page was workbench-first (B8a), the underwriting list/board was ZOHO-FIRST (`z?.closingDate ?? r.closingDate` — a stale Zoho date could win over the fresh Finmo-synced workbench one), and the deal-room header read the workbench date alone; `lib/closing-date.ts resolveClosingDate(workbench, zoho)` is the one answer all three now share, `getDealCloseout` gained `Closing_Date` as the header fallback, F053107 renders + sorts dated everywhere (+4 tests). THE PUBLISHING MODEL (migration `20260718180000`, applied live to FOXCA `skfeivzhqvrefnkqjwtj`, verified): three tables (`client_scenarios`/`client_offers`/`client_letters`), RLS deny-all + grants revoked + security-definer functions, mirroring the client-links posture EXACTLY. Admin writes demand `p_operator_secret` (the SAME sha256 client-links carries, in one private helper `client_presentation_secret_ok` — NO new secret; `FOXCA_OPERATOR_SECRET` already unlocks it). Client reads (`*_for_token`) are keyed by the link TOKEN HASH, joining `client_links` + filtering revoked/expired, returning only PUBLISHED content — so the public anon key cannot enumerate a deal's published content by guessing Zoho ids. SNAPSHOTS OVER REFERENCES: `figures`/`snapshot` are FROZEN at publish time, so a later rate change / re-grade / edited quote never rewrites a page a client already saw. `lib/client-presentation-store.ts` is the FOXCA twin (operator-secret admin writes demo-blocked; token-hash client reads not demo-guarded — the client page short-circuits to the fixture first). TASK 1 (Scenarios): `computeScenario`/`buildScenarioSnapshot` (lib/client-presentation.ts) reuse the EXISTING mortgage engine (`monthlyPayment` + `buildSchedule` + `paymentBreakdown`, semi-annual compounding — golden-tested to the cent anchors 2908.02 + 2999.58), never re-derived; an unpublishable computation returns `missing[]` naming what to fix and NEVER reaches the client; the client sees published scenarios side by side (plain line items, labelled estimates). TASK 2 (Offers with the disclosed grade): `config/offer-rubric.ts` is ONE config home — weights rate 30 / prepayment 20 / penalty 20 / portability 10 / fees 10 / flexibility 10, letter A≥85/B≥70/C≥55/D, coverage gate ≥70. THE HONESTY RULE, by construction: a component scores only on CITED TRUTH (the quote's own fields, or an APPROVED lender-wide knowledge claim via `getKnowledgeClaims`; `toRubricClaims` drops pending + program-scoped); no truth → "not on file", contributes nothing; the letter grade shows only at ≥70 gradeable, else "grade pending · N of 100 on file"; nothing invented, no gap averaged around. Only `penalty_methodology`/`ird_comparison_basis` is emitted today, so MOST offers grade "incomplete" until the knowledge base carries prepayment/portability/fees/flexibility claims — the honest state, forward-compatible (the rubric reads those topics and lights up with zero code change). REACHABLE-BOUNDARY finding: every weight is a multiple of 10, so the brief's "69 points" is illustrative; the reachable gate is 60 (withhold) / 70 (show), golden-tested. Michael picks from a deduped best-per-lender/term/type/class pick list (`buildOfferPickList`); the route re-fetches the approved quote + claims and builds the snapshot+grade SERVER-SIDE (the client sends only a quote id). TASK 3 (the letter, purchase only): `lib/preapproval-pdf.ts` mints a deterministic PDF (same pdf-lib habits as rates/savings) from the entered terms (max price / rate / rate-hold expiry / conditions / optional first name), reproducible from the FROZEN snapshot alone; append-only, re-mint supersedes; the client sees a Download card while the hold is live (`letterIsValid`), a warm "expired, reach out" once it passes, nothing once superseded; the download route (`/portal/file/[token]/letter`, public sub-path) reads the frozen snapshot — no client-triggered generation. Authoring: `components/admin/ClientPresentationCard.tsx` (one Section in the deal room, type-only model imports so node:crypto never enters the client bundle; every mutation POSTs to a gated `client.presentation.manage` route, demo-refused, then router.refresh). DEMO: admin lists resolve from fixtures with zero real reads; the demo client file carries scenarios + offers (one grade-complete A, one "grading incomplete") + a valid letter; every presentation write DemoWriteBlocked (asserted); the banned-verdict sweep stays green. RENDER PROOF, BOTH WIDTHS (blessed demo pattern — dev server, forged `fox_demo` cookie under the local SESSION_SECRET, the PUBLIC client page, zero real reads): the client demo page rendered the letter card, three graded offers (First National A itemised 30/30…, MCAP C, RMG "Grade pending · 30 of 100 on file"), the disclosed rubric, scenarios side by side, single-lime Call — zero horizontal overflow at 1280 AND 375 (measured). The client page read only Zoho's empty field. FIX: the page now sources the closing date from the workbench (resolve the deal by `zoho_potential_id` → `deals.closing_date`), falling back to Zoho's field; a bad `${iso}` concat is also guarded (`iso.slice(0,10)` so a timestamp never breaks `formatClosing`). The upload guidance stays beneath; the page still never hosts uploads; a failed request read falls back to the guidance text, never an error. ### Prior note — the toast fix (deploys must announce themselves)
THE LESSON FIRST, and it is the standing one: **unit tests proved the decision logic while the production triggers were unreachable — a feature of this class is only proven by a live deploy.** The B2b new-version toast never fired across two deploys, in two layers: (1) `public/sw.js` was a STATIC file with a hardcoded `SW_VERSION = 'fox-s9-v1'`, so its bytes were identical every deploy — and a browser fires `updatefound`/`controllerchange` (the only events the toast can hear) ONLY when the fetched sw.js differs byte-for-byte from the installed one; (2) nothing ever called `reg.update()` and Next's client routing never triggers the browser's own check, so an idle tab waited on the ~24h check. FIX: the worker moved OUT of public/ to `worker/sw.js` as a TEMPLATE carrying `__SW_VERSION__` (NEVER hardcode it again — `lib/sw-source.ts swBody()` THROWS on a missing placeholder, which under the build-time route fails the build rather than shipping a mute worker); NEW `app/sw.js/route.ts` (`dynamic='force-static'`) serves it at the unchanged root path with the deploy identity stamped in — `VERCEL_GIT_COMMIT_SHA` (platform-provided, NO new env var) → `BUILD_TIME` (already baked by next.config) → a stable `dev` literal so a dev server does not churn; a redeploy of the SAME commit is deliberately the same version (identical code is not a new version). `ServiceWorkerRegister` gained the missing TRIGGERS: `reg.update()` once after registration settles, every 10 minutes (`UPDATE_CHECK_INTERVAL_MS`), and on `visibilitychange` when visible, all torn down on unmount; the toast's decision rules and the never-auto-reload rule are UNTOUCHED (a check only ASKS). `scheduleUpdateChecks(check, host)` takes an INJECTED host because this repo has no jsdom — that is why the triggers are pure. FINDINGS: STATIC_CACHE already rode SW_VERSION and the activate handler already deleted every non-current cache (the brief's "add that if missing" was already satisfied — no leak, verified live); Clerk's middleware matcher excludes any path with an extension, so `/sw.js` needs NO publicRoutes entry. PROVEN, not assumed: `.next/server/app/sw.js.body` exists with a stamped version + `/sw.js` in the prerender manifest (so the fs read happens at BUILD, never at request time) and `route.js.nft.json` traces `worker/sw.js` as belt-and-braces; two consecutive builds shipped DIFFERENT bytes; live dev restart (a new BUILD_TIME = a stand-in deploy) made the REAL toast appear with exactly ONE cache left; the component's own `reg.update()` fired exactly once on a visible `visibilitychange` and zero while hidden. ADVERSARIAL REVIEW (5 lenses x 3 refuters) changed five things, TWO of them my own guard: the placeholder guard was defeated by the worker's own PROSE naming `__SW_VERSION__` (it now matches the DECLARATION — a guard a comment can satisfy is not a guard), and NOTHING tested the route, so gutting GET to serve the template unstamped kept 647 green and restored the original bug (a route test now closes it); plus a pre-existing security-test vacuity (the isCacheable invoked-not-declared count was satisfied by the header comment — now strips comments), two tests that read ambient env through JS default params (reproduced failing under a real SHA; now pass explicit sentinels), and a FALSE comment claiming next.config inlines the SHA (proven from the compiled bundle: only BUILD_TIME inlines; the SHA resolves ONLY because the route is force-static). swBody also now rejects a version that is not a bare identifier (it is stamped into a string literal). MUTATION-TESTED, since green tests are what failed us: unstamped GET -> 1 red, hardcoded SW_VERSION -> 9 red, interval dropped -> 1 red, isCacheable removed -> 1 red. 653 tests. **The real acceptance is the NEXT deploy: an open, untouched tab should toast within ~10 minutes.** See the 2026-07-17 toast-fix ledger entry + docs/toast-fix-2026-07-17.md.

  non-destructively by decoding the `roles` claim of an app-only token — the
  tenant's own statement of what the app may do. Granted roles are exactly
  `["Calendars.Read"]`. No write. So event creation is a MARKED STUB, and Azure
  grant steps for Michael are in docs/booking-engine-session-one-2026-07-27.md
  (App registrations → API permissions → Application permissions →
  Calendars.ReadWrite → **Grant admin consent**, which is the click that matters).
  NO Azure change was attempted; no secret value was printed anywhere.
- **THE STUB IS CAPABILITY-GATED, NOT HARDCODED** (the design choice that matters):
  `lib/booking/outlook.ts` reads the same `roles` claim at runtime and refuses
  BEFORE firing a doomed request. So no confusing 403s in the logs, the refusal
  reason is specific and reaches the booking row's `calendar_detail`, and the day
  the grant lands it starts writing with ZERO code change and no new env var (the
  attempt-and-fallback rule this repo already follows for workbench grants).
  Every booking meanwhile is REAL with `calendar_status='pending_retry'` + reason,
  which is session two's reconcile queue, already correctly populated.
- LOUD DEVIATION, "service-role writes only": THERE IS NO FOXCA SERVICE-ROLE KEY
  (only the anon key, shared with public form intake, plus FOXCA_OPERATOR_SECRET).
  Minting one would reverse the deliberate 2026-07-09 decision that deleted the
  workbench's. So this follows the ruling already written into
  20260717150000_client_links.sql: RLS on, no policies, grants revoked, five
  narrow security-definer functions each demanding `p_operator_secret` — strictly
  NARROWER than service-role (five operations, not a schema). The public visitor
  never reaches Supabase; the Next server makes every call.
- MIGRATIONS (new files only, nothing existing touched): `20260727160000_booking_engine.sql`
  (booking_hosts / calendar_connections / booking_event_types / booking_hours /
  booking_overrides / bookings; RLS + revokes; `booking_config_for`,
  `booking_availability_inputs`, `booking_create`, `booking_mark_calendar`,
  `booking_get`) and `20260727160100_booking_seed_mike.sql` (host `mike`, the
  Outlook row seeded `status='read_only'` with a NULL write target — the Step 0
  finding written into the DATA — four event types, Mon-Fri 9 to 5). Every table
  keyed by `agent_id` from the first migration; the value is Michael's canonical
  workbench `agents.id` `a0000000-...-0001`, resolved live by email, copied in as
  a VALUE so there is no runtime workbench dependency.
- SEEDED TYPES: discovery-call 15 / strategy-session 45 / smm-strategy-call 30 /
  signing-review 20. **DURATIONS ARE THE BRIEF'S AND ARE UNVERIFIED** — browsing to
  foxmortgage.zohobookings.com is blocked by policy, so Michael's live types could
  not be read. He confirms or corrects them; from session four they are editable
  in the dashboard, not in a migration.
- DESIGN DECISIONS, each stated because each is a real choice: (1) AVAILABILITY
  FAILS CLOSED — an unreadable calendar offers NO times and shows an honest line
  pointing at phone and email, because offering times we cannot verify are free
  double-books a client. (2) THE PREFILL TOKEN CARRIES RECORD IDS ONLY — a signed
  token is signed, not encrypted, so a name or email in it is a name or email in
  the URL; contact prefill moves to a server-side lookup in session two, and a
  test asserts the payload keys and that no `@` appears. (3) THE RATE LIMITER IS
  BEST EFFORT and says so — in-process state is never for correctness here; the
  real guards are the DB rules. (4) TWO REDUNDANT CONFLICT CHECKS on purpose: the
  engine re-check enforces every rule, the SQL re-check + partial unique index on
  `(agent_id, starts_at) where status='booked'` enforce the two that must hold
  under concurrency (in practice a taken slot refuses as `slot_not_offered`;
  `slot_taken` is the true microsecond race). (5) `bookings.local_date` is an
  ADDITION to the brief's column list — the host-local date computed server-side,
  which makes every per-day rule DST-correct with zero timezone math in SQL.
- BUILD: pure + tested `lib/booking/{time,availability,validate,tokens,types}.ts`
  (timezone math hand-rolled two-pass because this repo vendors no date library);
  server-only `lib/booking/{calendar,outlook,google,store,engine,rate-limit}.ts`;
  `app/book/[host]/[eventType]/{page.tsx,BookingFlow.tsx}`; `/api/book/slots` +
  `/api/book/confirm`; five middleware publicRoutes entries (both `/book` AND
  `/book/(.*)` per the pair convention, plus each API path literally).
- VERIFIED LIVE against Michael's REAL calendar, not a fixture: full days show 30
  open slots, days with real meetings show 23 to 26, Jul 28 starts 10:15 AM
  because a real event blocks that morning, weekends and today correctly absent;
  the same day showed 25 open for a 45-minute type and 28 for a 15-minute one. A
  REAL booking through the REAL form stored 2:00 PM Toronto as `18:00Z`, correct
  `local_date`, phone normalized `+16475550142`, consent + timestamp, a 64-char
  token HASH (raw never stored), and `pending_retry` with the honest permission
  reason. The slot then vanished from the public list ALONG WITH 1:45 PM (the
  5-minute buffer blocks the neighbour) — 28 → 25, exactly the three slots the
  padded interval covers. Every refusal path exercised (slot taken, duplicate
  across different email CASING, off-grid time, outside hours, past time, missing
  phone, bad email, missing required intake answer, honeypot, unknown host) and
  after all of it the table held exactly ONE row — every refusal stored nothing.
  Anon posture proven: all six tables `42501`, every function refuses without the
  secret and answers with it. Zero horizontal overflow at 1280 AND 375, zero
  console errors. The proof booking was DELETED (state, not an audit trail).
- A SELF-CAUGHT BUG WORTH RECORDING: my own copy-gate sweep failed on my own
  files, correctly — but the sweep was ALSO wrong, because `!` and `;` are
  ordinary TypeScript. It now strips comments and extracts only JSX text and
  quoted strings, plus a companion test asserting the extractor really finds page
  copy so it cannot pass vacuously.
- GOOGLE, recorded in `lib/booking/google.ts` so nobody discovers it late: OAuth
  verification for calendar scopes is a LEAD TIME OF WEEKS TO MONTHS (verified
  domain, privacy policy, demo video, security questionnaire, and for restricted
  scopes a paid third-party assessment); unverified apps are capped at 100 test
  users behind a warning screen. If agent two runs on Google Workspace, START THE
  SUBMISSION A FULL QUARTER AHEAD. Domain-wide delegation avoids it but only
  inside one Workspace tenant.
- CUTOVER INVENTORY (session four's job, mapped early as a bonus): `lib/contact.ts:31`
  is the ONLY booking-URL literal in this repo, feeding 8 consumer surfaces; the
  vendor name is in user-visible copy at 4 files (`'Schedule time via Zoho Bookings'`);
  `RENEWAL_CALENDAR_URL` + `COMMS_BOOKING_LINK` live in the WORKBENCH as env vars;
  `[CalendarLink]` has zero code references in either repo (a Zoho-side merge
  token); `reviewUrl`/`COMMS_REVIEW_LINK` is the silent twin to cut over in the
  same pass; two demo fixtures hardcode the pre-cutover held reason.
- Guardrails held: no new services or vendors (Graph, Supabase, Resend, n8n only),
  no `vercel env` commands, no Azure changes, no new env var (prefill signing
  reuses SESSION_SECRET), no existing migration touched, `/book` unlinked from
  public nav, copy gate on every client string, PII discipline (synthetic design
  cast + example.com only, proof row deleted). See
  docs/booking-engine-session-one-2026-07-27.md.

### 2026-07-20 — Ask Fox truncation handling and honest errors (portal lane)
- Base `c566159`. Fixes the logged production crash `[agent] turn failed: Unterminated string in JSON at position 6000` + hardens Ask Fox's error handling. Backend only (loop + one tool helper), no UI change. 921 → **926 tests** (+5), tsc clean, `next build` green, shell lime audit green. Committed NOT pushed.
- FINDINGS-FIRST, LOUD DEVIATION: the failure is NOT a model max_tokens/stop_reason truncation (the brief's framing). The loop never JSON.parses the model reply (the SDK does); stop_reason is already checked (refusal/max_tokens/tool_use). Position 6000 = the ONLY `.slice(0,6000)` in the codebase: `lib/agent/tools.ts` `knowledge_lookup` capped the lender profile with `JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(d.profile,null,0).slice(0,6000))` — slicing JSON mid-token then re-parsing throws for any profile whose compact JSON >6000 chars. The handler has no try/catch, so the throw hit the loop catch, which logged the failure AND emitted the misleading "could not reach the model." AGENT_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS=16000 is generous, NOT tight (not raised).
- FIX: (1) NEW `cappedProfile` (pure, exported, tools.ts) — caps a value's size WITHOUT slice-then-reparse (small → the structured object; oversized → a valid `{truncated,note,text}` object with the first N chars as text; never throws); `knowledge_lookup` uses it. (2) the loop wraps the tool dispatch so a thrown tool becomes a failed `tool_result` (is_error) and the turn CONTINUES — no single tool bug crashes a turn. (3) the catch now differentiates THREE honest cases (plain words, NO semicolons, all saying "Nothing was written"): could-not-reach (network/API/auth/rate-limit), and a NEW could-not-read branch (`isUnreadableReplyError` — a JSON/SyntaxError, distinct from could-not-reach); the answer-was-cut-off is the existing max_tokens branch. (4) on ANY caught error the loop returns `text:''` so a failed turn never persists a partial as the reply. Semicolons removed from the catch copy + the iteration-ceiling copy.
- INTERPRETATION stated: "never partially rendered/written" applies to UNREADABLE/failed replies (the catch path, text:''); a genuine max_tokens cutoff is VALID model text kept with an honest cut-off note (the brief's own "fail with honest copy about the answer being cut off"), not discarded — discarding a large mostly-complete answer would be user-hostile.
- VERIFIED: 926 tests (`tests/agent.test.ts` +5) — cappedProfile reproduces the exact "Unterminated string at position 6000" throw of the OLD approach and proves the fix round-trips as JSON; the loop honest-read test throws the exact production SyntaxError shape and asserts `error` contains "could not read" not "could not reach", `text===''`, "Nothing was written", no semicolon, and an in-stream error event; a tool-throw test proves the turn survives with the model's reply and a failed tool log; isUnreadableReplyError classification. DEMO render (dev-Clerk ephemeral TEST admin created + DELETED): Ask Fox in demo answered a long question with the canned `[Demo]` reply (route short-circuits before any model/workbench/Zoho call — zero real reads), no error copy, zero horizontal overflow.
- DEFERRED, LOUD: the LIVE model turn can't run locally — no ANTHROPIC_API_KEY (by design) + the build guardrail forbids setting it, and the dev Clerk instance can't mint the `gates` token `knowledge_lookup` needs. Proven by the unit tests (which reproduce the exact failure and the honest path) + the demo render; the live model turn is Michael's step (the Agent-session precedent). Guardrails: readonly workbench untouched, no gate/authz/migration/env change, no new tool or send path, copy rules on the new error strings, PII discipline (TEST admin only, deleted). See docs/ask-fox-truncation-2026-07-20.md.

### 2026-07-20 — The Today calendar band goes live (Microsoft Graph, read-only)
- Base `18b79f1`. The "Your day" calendar card stops being a connect state and renders today's real Microsoft calendar. Read-only, server-side, fail-soft. NO Graph write anywhere. 897 → **914 tests** (+17), tsc clean, `next build` green, shell lime audit green. Committed NOT pushed.
- FINDINGS FIRST: greenfield (no prior Graph plumbing); `server-only` is a comment convention here (no npm pkg) so it is enforced by a static TEST; all four `MS_*` vars present (two had a stray leading space → `msEnv()` trims); a live probe confirmed creds + the response shape (with `Prefer: outlook.timezone="America/Toronto"`, `calendarView` returns NAIVE Toronto wall-clock start/end). No load-bearing wrong assumption; one honest copy tweak (the connect state's "Coming soon" → "Ask your admin to set it up" now that the build is live).
- NEW `lib/ms-calendar.ts` (server-only): client-credentials token (in-proc cache to 60s-before-expiry, never logged), `calendarView` for the exact Toronto day, slim `GraphEventLite` (no attendees/bodies/join URLs), a 60s in-proc lites cache (the "short revalidate"; failures never cached), and the PURE mapper (`mapCalendarEvents`/`localMinutes`/`localOffsetMinutes`/`fmtClock`/`torontoNowMinutes`) — status+sort are DATE-AWARE (minutes relative to today's midnight) while labels use the event's own wall clock. `getTodayCalendar` NEVER throws (missing env → `{configured:false}`; any failure caught → `{configured:true,ok:false}`), so it is `Promise.all`-safe. Result type mirrors `UwResult`.
- BAND (`components/admin/today/YourDay.tsx`): four states (connect / "Calendar is not available right now." / teaching empty / the time-ordered list — muted past, ink-navy accent + "now" marker, all-day neutral). NO lime. `app/portal/admin/page.tsx` fetches it in the existing `Promise.all` and threads `calendar` to `YourDay`.
- DEMO: `demoCalendarLites` (in `lib/demo-fixtures.ts`, obviously fictional, all four states) through the SAME pure mapper; `tests/demo.test.ts` asserts events with zero fetch. TYPE-ONLY import of `GraphEventLite` into demo-fixtures so `ms-calendar` runtime-imports the builder with no cycle.
- SECURITY: secrets/UPN in env only, read ONLY in `lib/ms-calendar.ts`, never NEXT_PUBLIC, never logged (status codes only) or in a URL query; `tests/ms-calendar.test.ts` asserts no client file imports the module, the module reads no client-exposed env, and no `NEXT_PUBLIC_MS_` exists. Report note: tenant-wide `Calendars.Read` today; an ApplicationAccessPolicy can scope it to Michael's mailbox (his later hardening pass).
- ADVERSARIAL REVIEW (4 dims × refuting verify; 5 raised / **2 CONFIRMED, both fixed** + regressions / 3 refuted): (HIGH) the calendarView window was offset-less, which Graph reads as UTC (the Prefer header only sets response rendering), so the window was the UTC day shifted 4-5h — today's evening events dropped and yesterday's late ones leaked as stale rows (a daytime test can't surface it — why the probe passed); FIXED to UTC instants of the Toronto day via `torontoDayStartISO` (DST-correct), verified live that the corrected window returns today's event with no stale leak. (MED) `localMinutes` discarded the date so cross-midnight/multi-day timed events got wrong status/sort; FIXED via `localOffsetMinutes` + regression tests (overnight + multi-day, from today's AND tomorrow's view).
- PROVEN LIVE (blessed pattern: local dev, dev-Clerk ephemeral TEST admin created + DELETED in-session, read-only): real event ("11:00 AM · Recurring Mortgage Meeting", correct Toronto time/order, clean-tab zero console errors) at 1280 + 375; demo multi-state at 1280 + 375; BOTH failure paths end-to-end (env blanked → connect state; bad secret → the quiet line + server log `[ms-calendar] token HTTP 401`), the rest of Today intact in both, `.env.local` backed up + restored clean; zero horizontal overflow at every width. The render proofs are of the pre-fix code; the two fixes change only the query bounds + date-aware status/sort, so same-day rendering (the screenshots) is unchanged, and the fixes are covered by unit tests + the live query-window probe.
- Closing ritual: this ledger + the header note, config/changelog.ts, roadmap (Today v1 row + a calendar-band item), docs/ms-calendar-2026-07-20.md. The docs/input-commit-2026-07-18.md → -2026-07-20.md rename was ALREADY done in the prior (task two-way) session — verified no stale `input-commit-2026-07-18` pointer remains (only historical ledger prose naming the rename). ### 2026-07-20 — Task two-way: complete a Zoho task from Today (the portal's first Zoho write)
- Base `e92f9ab`. The Tasks card on Today gains a checkbox that completes the task in Zoho; Zoho stays the source of truth (no local task state can drift — a completed task drops from the next `getTasksDue` read, which already excludes Completed). 892 → **897 tests** (+5), tsc clean, `next build` green, shell lime test green. FOXCA migration `20260720120000` APPLIED LIVE. No new env var. Committed NOT pushed.
- FINDINGS FIRST: (1) **write capability CONFIRMED** with a disposable round trip (create → read → update→Completed → reopen → DELETE, all success, deleted, real tasks untouched) — the credential is NOT read-only, no scope change, the read-only contingency does not apply; auth = the existing `getZohoToken()`. (2) `getTasksDue` already filters `Status !== 'Completed'`. (3) a `completeZohoTask` existed with zero app callers → generalized to `setZohoTaskStatus` (+ the non-success check a Zoho PUT can return) + a new `getZohoTask` (read the prior status). (4) "one server action" = the house gated POST route; a verified Clerk admin session is the SOLE way in, NO machine path.
- THE PATH: `app/api/portal/admin/tasks/[id]/status/route.ts`, `{action:'complete'|'reopen'}`, new admin-only key `tasks.complete` (config/authority.ts + label + authority.test). Order: gate → demo 403 (FIRST, before any Zoho touch) → validate → act → audit. complete = read current status → record as prior → set Completed (idempotent no-op if already Completed). reopen = restore the status the most recent complete recorded (`lib/tasks.ts reopenTargetFrom`, pure, tested; safe default `Not Started`). Both audit to FOXCA `task_action_events` (actor `gate.user.email`, task, action, prev/new status, result, ts). Loud failure: a Zoho write throw → failed attempt audited + 502 "The write did not land"; the card reverts + one sentence. A landed Zoho write is never reported failed over a best-effort audit hiccup (the success-path audit is try/caught).
- THE CARD: `components/admin/today/TaskList.tsx` (`'use client'`) extracted from YourDay — a real `<button role="checkbox">` (Space/Enter complete it; seeds the keyboard pattern, nothing more), optimistic tick + pending, ~10s Undo → reopen, `router.refresh()` after the window drops the completed row, honest revert + reason on failure. No lime (walked by the shell audit, green). `getZohoTask`/`setZohoTaskStatus` demo-blocked (`tests/demo.test.ts`: read returns null + writes throw DemoWriteBlocked, zero fetch).
- STORAGE: `supabase/migrations/20260720120000_task_action_events.sql` (table + `task_action_record`/`_events_for_task`/`_events_recent`, all plpgsql security definer requiring `p_operator_secret` via `foxca_operator_secret_ok`, RLS on, grants revoked — the `renewal_events` pattern, hardened from the start). `lib/task-events-store.ts` = the FOXCA twin of renewals-store; nothing deletes; append-only trail of actions, never task state.
- VERIFIED: 897 tests (`tests/tasks.test.ts` pure helpers, `tests/demo.test.ts` +1 task read/write block, `tests/authority.test.ts` admin-only baseline); tsc clean; build green (`/api/portal/admin/tasks/[id]/status` registered); shell lime audit green. LIVE (all via scratchpad harnesses, TEST/disposable only, deleted): anon posture (table select 401; `task_action_record` uncallable without the secret, succeeds with it → uuid; TEST row deleted, table empty); demo BOTH widths (ephemeral dev-Clerk TEST admin created + DELETED — checkbox renders, click → `POST …/tasks/demo-t-1/status → 403` before any Zoho touch, card reverts + "Demo mode is read-only", zero console errors, zero horizontal overflow at 1280 and 375); live write proof — route round trip (complete → Zoho **Completed** + audit `complete | prev=Not Started | new=Completed | ok`; reopen → Zoho **Not Started** + audit `reopen | prev=Completed | new=Not Started | ok`) AND the PHYSICAL card cycle (a disposable given a far-past due date so it sorted into the rendered rows: click the checkbox → the done state + Undo button → Zoho Completed + audit ok; click Undo → reopen → row back to open → Zoho Not Started + audit ok; the ~10s window then router.refresh-drops the row) AND the honest-failure path live from the card (an earlier click hit a transient Zoho OAuth token rate limit my harnesses had exhausted → checkbox reverted + one plain sentence + a `failed: …` audit row). The disposable Zoho tasks + TEST admin were DELETED; the proof audit rows genuinely landed in live FOXCA (a SELECT before cleanup returned all five) but were then ALSO deleted via the Supabase MCP service role — a MISTAKE, now corrected by the new standing rule "Audit trails are append-only, including during proofs" (§ house rules; audit rows stay from now on).
- Closing ritual: this ledger + the header note, config/changelog.ts, roadmap (Today v1 row), docs/task-two-way-2026-07-20.md; also renamed docs/input-commit-2026-07-18.md → docs/input-commit-2026-07-20.md (both CLAUDE.md references updated). ### 2026-07-18 — Patch: scenario inputs commit on blur/Enter (no search per keystroke)
- Base `5f3ac1a`. The admin Rates scenario view fired the match engine per digit: the two `type="number"` inputs (Mortgage amount + Property value, `RatesScenario.tsx`) called `setScenario` → `router.replace` on every `onChange`, so typing a 7-digit amount = 7 force-dynamic RSC refetches of `getRateQuotesFull` (~1257 rows ×2) + 7 full-book client recomputes. FIX: NEW `lib/input-commit.ts commitNumericInput` (pure blur/Enter parser, unit-tested, tolerant of `1,500,000`/`$928,000`, null-never-NaN) + NEW `components/admin/CommittedNumberField.tsx` (local text while typing, commits once on blur/Enter, resyncs on external value change); the two inputs adopt it, each `onCommit` guarded against a no-op re-search.
- FINDINGS-FIRST sweep (parallel readers): the ONLY per-keystroke defect is those two inputs. The B9 client qualification explorer does NOT share it (pure O(1) in-render computeQualification, zero network — live slider feedback is the intended UX, left unchanged); cmd-K is already debounced (180ms + AbortController); Ask Fox submits on Enter; RatesBrowser/RatesLenders/Partners/Provision filters are client-side useMemo over loaded props; the RatesScenario selects/checkboxes navigate once per selection, not a keystroke. WRONG-ASSUMPTION in the brief, stated loudly: the server refetch is data-independent (`getRateQuotesFull` is agent-scoped) — each keystroke re-read the identical book and the matching is entirely client-side; the same wasteful refetch on every select change is a pre-existing inefficiency (follow-up: decouple the quotes fetch from searchParams), out of this patch's scope.
- STANDING RULE added (CLAUDE.md Input-commit discipline): no keystroke triggers a network call or heavy recompute; commit on blur/Enter or debounce ≥600ms with cancellation; scoped to matching/fetching inputs, not the pure-local live recompute. Changelog + roadmap updated.
- PROOF, live: dev-Clerk ephemeral TEST admin (`commit-proof+clerk_test@example.com`, a Clerk test email so dev code 424242 clears the email-code 2FA) created via the Clerk backend API, signed in through Clerk JS, DELETED before session end (0 remaining). On the real Rates view, typing `1500000` digit-by-digit produced ZERO navigations (`location.search` unchanged, RSC log carries no partial `amount=`); blur fired exactly ONE RSC commit `…&amount=1500000&_rsc=…`, summary updated to `$1.5M`; superseded RSC fetches abort (net::ERR_ABORTED) so a stale result never lands. 860 → **865 tests** (+5), tsc clean, `next build` green, shell lime test green. No migration, no fox-underwriting/Zoho changes. Committed NOT pushed. See docs/input-commit-2026-07-20.md.

### 2026-07-18 — FOXCA-wide operator-secret hardening (micro-session)
- Enumerated every SECURITY DEFINER function in FOXCA (`skfeivzhqvrefnkqjwtj`, ~90) and ruled each. The FOXCA anon key is shared with public form-intake, so every admin function `grant execute to anon` was a de facto service account. Migration `20260718190000`, APPLIED LIVE. 818 → **823 tests**, tsc clean, `next build` green. No new env, no fox-underwriting changes, no Zoho writes. Committed NOT pushed.
- **65 functions hardened** with a required `p_operator_secret` (the client_link/client_presentation pattern EXACTLY — SAME `FOXCA_OPERATOR_SECRET`, old signatures DROPPED first per the overload trap, every `language sql` fn rewritten to plpgsql to raise 42501). ONE shared DB guard `public.foxca_operator_secret_ok(text)` (revoked from all roles) carries the sha256; ONE shared store helper `lib/foxca-secret.ts foxcaOperatorSecret()` (throw-if-unset) threads it through every store. Families: form-intake (ack/stats/failures), Ask Fox (agent_*), client constraints + pins, compliance (all), notifications (producer + user writes), people (provision/offboard), renewals, saved-scenario writes, savings-analysis log, SMM (all), view-as; PLUS `client_link_event_record` (closes the B7-P audit-forge residual, previously left open). `savings_analysis_recent` + `smm_backfill_events_recent` are hardened in the DB but have no caller (unwired) — no store change.
- **STAY OPEN (9, ruled with reasons):** token-keyed client-page flow (`client_link_resolve`, `client_link_touch`, `client_scenarios_for_token`, `client_offers_for_token`, `client_letter_for_token` — the 256-bit token hash is the per-record secret); `mark_form_submission` (public form-capture, anon by design); the user-scoped reads `notifications_list_for_user`, `notification_prefs_get`, `saved_scenarios_list_for_user`. Already secured (unchanged, 16): the client_link_* + client_letter/offer/scenario_* + `client_presentation_secret_ok`.
- **JUDGMENT, stated:** the brief's read exemption is *user-scoped OR token-keyed* reads only; admin LIST-reads (compliance register with holder names, view-as logs, staff provisions/offboards, savings analyses, SMM data, failed-form PII) are neither and expose business/PII/audit data off the public anon key, so they are hardened too — an extension of "admin-mutating" to "admin operations that touch business/PII data," reasoned in docs/foxca-hardening-2026-07-18.md. User-scoped WRITES (notification read-state, saved scenarios) are hardened as well (writes, not client-flow writes; an anon-key holder could otherwise mutate any user's state by passing their clerk id).
- **STORES:** `lib/status.ts` (form-intake reads converted GET → POST so the secret never rides a URL query string; ack gains the secret), `lib/client-links-store.ts` (event_record), `lib/compliance.ts`, `lib/notifications-store.ts`, `lib/people-store.ts`, `lib/renewals-store.ts`, `lib/saved-scenarios-store.ts`, `lib/smm-store.ts`, `lib/constraints-store.ts`, `lib/agent/store.ts` — every hardened rpc call gains `p_operator_secret: foxcaOperatorSecret()`; the 3 open reads untouched. (The 8 uniform stores were threaded by a per-file parallel workflow; verified by the source-coverage test + a balanced-brace coverage grep.)
- **DEPLOY-ORDERING, LOUD (B7-P pattern, FOXCA-wide scale):** dropping the old signatures means the currently-deployed code (calling without the secret) errors on EVERY hardened function until this session's code deploys — the WHOLE FOXCA admin surface (compliance, notifications, people, renewals, SMM, Ask Fox, constraints, saved scenarios, view-as, savings log, Status form-intake) is DOWN (graceful error states, not crashes) between apply and deploy. The client status page + token-keyed presentation + public form capture are unaffected. **PUSH + DEPLOY IMMEDIATELY.**
- VERIFIED: tsc clean, build green, 823 tests (`tests/foxca-secret-hardening.test.ts` +5). LIVE POSTURE proven as the anon role (python + PostgREST): every hardened function refuses without the secret (42501 operator secret required), succeeds with it, old un-secured signature gone (42883); re-enumeration confirms all 65 carry the guard. THE HONESTY RULE by construction: a component scores only on CITED TRUTH (the quote's own fields, or an APPROVED lender-wide knowledge claim via `getKnowledgeClaims`; `toRubricClaims` drops pending + program-scoped claims); no truth → "not on file", contributes nothing; the letter grade shows only at ≥70 gradeable, else "grade pending · N of 100 on file"; nothing invented, no gap averaged around. Only `penalty_methodology`/`ird_comparison_basis` is emitted today (consumed already by lib/knowledge-claims.ts), so most offers grade "incomplete" until the knowledge base carries prepayment/portability/fees/flexibility claims — the honest, forward-compatible state (the rubric reads those topics and lights up with zero code change). REACHABLE-BOUNDARY finding, stated: every weight is a multiple of 10, so the brief's "69 points" is illustrative; the reachable gate is 60 (withhold) / 70 (show), golden-tested. Michael picks from a deduped best-per-(lender,term,type,class) pick list (`buildOfferPickList`); the route re-fetches the approved quote + its lender's claims and builds the snapshot + grade SERVER-SIDE (the client sends only a quote id).

- TASK 1 (attribution): `DocumentsDesk` StateChip renders **"Approved in Finmo"** for a Finmo `status='approved'` (its token reflects Michael's accepts inside Finmo) — never a bare "Approved", never "by you" (the desk can't know which human clicked in Finmo). A human-`verified` condition still reads "Confirmed". The AI verdict line stays a separate draft. One-line change; regression fixture asserts no bare "Approved" renders.
- TASK 2 (deterministic freshness): NEW `config/doc-freshness.ts` — `FRESHNESS_DAYS` per-doc-kind table (ONE place, Michael-adjustable, drafts for sign-off: pay_stub/proof_of_pay 30; bank/mortgage/loc/letter_of_employment 60; benefit_statement(CCB) 90; property_tax/noa/t4/t1/id/void_cheque = null NO day window, content-date rules deferred to W2) + `freshnessKindFor` (commitment doc_kind authoritative; Finmo name classified by keyword, proof-of-pay before pay-stub) + `staleness(win, updatedAt, now)`. The model gained a `stale` overlay on `RequestCard` computed from the newest received file's `finmo_updated_at` vs an injected `now` (default Date.now(); tests pass a fixed now): over the window → amber "May be stale (uploaded N days ago)", `filter` forced to 'look', sorts JUST BELOW ai_flagged (new `rankOf`: flagged 0, stale 1, rest 2+). PRECEDENCE (tested, load-bearing): staleness NEVER hides/replaces/demotes the approval chip — both render side by side; done counts now key on `filter==='done'` so a stale-approved card is counted into look, not done. A commitment card has no honest received timestamp → never day-window-stale. No window / no timestamp → no flag, never a guess.
- TASK 3 (same-given-name disambiguation): section headers add relationship-where-known ("Lyntje (spouse)"), else a neutral ordinal, never fabricated. Ported the notes-layer precedence idea (`disambiguateBorrowerLabels`, fox-underwriting) but ADAPTED, stated loud: given-name headers + relationship-first + per-member (the notes layer is full-name + birth-year-first + all-or-nothing) — because `dob` is null on these rows (live: F053107's two "Lyntje Zinger" carry dob null, relationships family/spouse → "Lyntje (family)" + "Lyntje (spouse)"). `relationship` added to `getDealBorrowers` + `BorrowerRow` + `BorrowerInfo`; disambiguation keyed by finmo borrower id.
- VERIFIED: tsc clean; `next build` green; **744 tests** (from 720: +18 doc-freshness, +9 desk B6.3, +demo). Lime audit green, ZERO additions (staleness is amber, advisory). RENDER PROOFS (dev-instance, ephemeral TEST admin created + DELETED, demo synthetic data only): desktop desk — Bank Statement "✓ Approved in Finmo" + amber "May be stale (uploaded 89 days ago)" sorted just below the flagged LOE, pills All 12 / Waiting 4 / Needs your look 5 / Done 3, sections "Jordan (parent)" + "Jordan (spouse)"; 375px single-column zero horizontal overflow. Demo exited, TEST admin deleted, server stopped.
- ADVERSARIAL REVIEW (2 dimensions, verified): TWO confirmed findings, both fixed — (1) the attribution reached the StateChip but not the request-EXPANSION's reviewed line, still a bare "Approved <date>" → now "Approved in Finmo <date>" (in the pushed commit); (2) a classifier ordering bug — the broad `proof of income` pattern preceded NOA, so "Proof of income - Notice of Assessment" wrongly earned a 30-day window (false stale advisory on an annual tax doc) → fixed by classifying the no-window tax kinds (NOA/T4/T1) FIRST + narrowing proof-of-pay to "proof of pay", +2 regression tests (744 tests). Nothing else survived (stale line beside the chip; no UI em dashes; zero lime).
- W2 (content-date freshness, needs B7): extract document-internal dates per kind (tax year, current-year, on-face expiry), re-evaluate on a schedule, and the stale-to-chase handoff (a stale request → a one-tap re-request to the client).
- Guardrails: no writes anywhere; the freshness table is config not scattered constants; copy rules on every string (no em dashes, sentence case); lime rule intact; the Task 3 deviation named loud; no client names in the diff (synthetic cast + demo only; F053107 by ref); committed NOT pushed. See docs/desk-freshness-b63-2026-07-18.md.

### 2026-07-18 — B6.2: the request-centric documents desk (+ F053107 header/calc fixes)
- THE REFRAME: B6 rendered DOCUMENTS as the unit; the practice runs on Finmo document REQUESTS. This rebuilds the desk around the request (document_index) and fixes the two live F053107 defects Michael found. Presentation + one read-only data grant. Base `5cf0297`, 707 → **718 tests**.

So **fox-underwriting migration `0048_portal_readonly_document_index.sql`** (grant select + a portal_readonly SELECT policy, the 0034 document_pages pattern) — its OWN commit in the workbench repo, nothing else there touched — APPLIED LIVE to `rnupbdmpxfwsowiqhcqv` (verified: the same query now 200s). No analysis table needed a grant: the verdict lives on `conditions.presence_detail` (already granted); `documents` + `document_pages` already granted.
- FINDINGS (workbench-mapping agent + live F053107 read): (1) status vocab is RICHER than the 0020 comment — live shows `approved`/`for_review`/`requested`; `for_review` = received-awaiting-review; `has_src` mostly false, so **"received" = number_of_files>0 (or has_src), never a status token**. (3) Human approval per request: `document_index.status='approved'` is FINMO's approval → rendered "Approved" (never "by you"); a bridging condition `verified` → "Confirmed"; no portal per-request approve action exists (W2). - TASK 1 (`lib/documents-desk.ts` REWRITE → `buildRequestsDesk`, 30 tests; `DocumentsDesk.tsx` REWRITE, client): request cards, states waiting → received ("Ready for your look") → ai_flagged (amber, plain reason from rule_note, sorts first) → ai_passed ("Looks right") → reviewed (Approved/Confirmed); borrower sections (General first, per `finmo_borrower_id`, given names) each with progress; overall "N of M complete"; filter pills All/Waiting/Needs your look/Done with counts. Evidence renderer reparented, not rebuilt.

### 2026-07-17 — B6: the documents desk (request and review at a glance)
- THE SURFACE: the deal room's Documents subsection (`app/portal/admin/deals/[id]/page.tsx`, under the Underwriting phase). The received-documents `<table>` became a DESK of compact per-document cards in three groups — **Needs your eyes / Waiting on the client / Done** — so requested vs received vs reviewed reads in one glance. Presentation ONLY: no fetcher, gate, authz, route, or write changed; the desk reads the TWO reads the page already made (`getDealDocuments` + `getApprovedConditions`). Census untouched by construction.
- FINDINGS FIRST (inventory in docs/documents-desk-b6-2026-07-17.md §1): (1) the current area = the two uploaders + a synthetic banner + a plain received-documents table + the SectionFallback; NO per-row actions exist today (review/approve happens on Approvals + the conditions checklist), so the cards carry no invented buttons — the uploaders stay as the only actions. (2) `DocumentRow` carries `reviewStatus` + `provenance` but **NO verdict column** — the document-intelligence verdict lives on a CONDITION at `presenceDetail.analysis`, and it CARRIES a `document_id` (B5). So the pass/short/needs-review verdict IS reachable read-only today by a HARD id join (`analysis.document_id === document.id`) from data the page already fetches — no cross-repo plumbing. (3) DEVIATION, stated: "Waiting on the client" (requested-but-not-received) is NOT a `documents`-table row — it is an open document-condition (`docKind` set, `presence` needs_input/requested); the desk draws its three groups from BOTH existing reads, and the waiting cards deliberately overlap the conditions checklist as a read-only glance (the checklist stays the working surface). (4) `analysis.document_id` is not always stamped (B5 deferred) — so the join falls back to a UNIQUE `(docKind, borrowerId)` match, and DECLINES to attribute when ambiguous (never guessed). (5) `review_status` vocab observed = approved/reviewed/pending/rejected; unknown values route to Needs your eyes, never silently Done.
- THE MODEL: `lib/documents-desk.ts` (pure, unit-tested) `buildDocumentsDesk(documents, conditions)` → `{needsEyes, waiting, done, counts, isEmpty}`. Received doc: approved/reviewed → Done green; rejected → Done red; synthetic → Needs-eyes loud red (guardrail 20, never approvable); else pending → Needs-eyes, state chip AMBER "Needs attention" when a concerning verdict is attached else navy-outline "In review". Requested (condition) → Waiting gray "Requested", suppressed when a doc of the same (docKind,borrowerId) already arrived. Verdict tone mirrors ConditionsChecklist VERDICT_TONE (one vocabulary). Sort: synthetic → red → amber → plain within Needs-eyes; waiting by due asc; done by received desc.
- THE COMPONENT: `components/admin/deals/DocumentsDesk.tsx` (SERVER, presentation). Three grouped card grids (ds section header + count; `grid-cols-1 sm:grid-cols-2 xl:grid-cols-3`, single column at phone), each card = name / borrower·source / state chip / optional analysis chip ("Analysis (draft)" + as-of) / date / loud synthetic warning. NO lime (reviewing is work, not a queued decision) — StatusChip for green/amber/red/gray, an inline navy-outline span for received-pending. Empty state = the muted "No documents recorded on this file yet." line. Page wiring: `<table>` → `<DocumentsDesk>`, dropped the now-unused CELL_DATE import; uploaders + synthetic banner + SectionFallback + every gate UNCHANGED.
- READ CONTRACT (report §3, for a future workbench session, NOT blocking): (a) always stamp `analysis.document_id` so the desk never needs the ambiguity-guarded fallback; (b) if the workbench ever exposes a per-document `review_verdict` on the `documents` row (keyed by documents.id, freshness = on index/recompute), the card's analysis-chip slot renders it with ZERO design change — the card accommodates the chip whether or not a verdict is present.
- DEMO: added `demo-d-3` (t4_noa, pending) to `demoDealDocuments` and stamped `demo-cond-4`'s analysis `document_id='demo-d-3'`, so demo-deal-1's desk shows all three groups + the amber "Needs attention" state via the hard join; demo-deal-2 (no docs, one verified condition) shows the empty state. tests/demo.test.ts asserts all three groups + amber with ZERO real fetch.
- VERIFIED: tsc clean; `next build` green; **707 tests** (from 684: +22 documents-desk covering grouping/join/fallback-ambiguity/dedup/sort/empty, +1 demo). Lime audit (tests/shell.test.ts) green, ZERO additions.
- ADVERSARIAL REVIEW (3 dimensions — correctness / scope-honesty / design-copy, each finding independently verified): THREE confirmed, all fixed — two em-dash copy nits (the desk synthetic warning + the touched synthetic banner line), and ONE low-severity correctness bug: a synthetic (or rejected) document was counted as "arrived" in the waiting-dedup set and wrongly suppressed an open Waiting request for the same kind (a synthetic stand-in has NOT arrived, guardrail 20; a rejection leaves the request open) — fixed so only a REAL non-rejected document suppresses a Waiting card, +2 regression tests. Everything else refuted (reviewed→Done green is a deliberate improvement; raw as_of matches the ConditionsChecklist convention). RENDER PROOFS (dev-instance pattern, dev Clerk ephemeral TEST admin created + DELETED in-session, demo synthetic data only, screenshots referenced not committed): desktop all-three-groups+amber (demo-deal-1), 375px single-column with ZERO horizontal overflow (scrollWidth-clientWidth===0), and the empty state (demo-deal-2). Demo entered + EXITED, dev server stopped.
- Guardrails held: presentation over existing reads (no fetcher/gate/authz/route/write/env change — diff is the two new files + the Documents subsection + demo fixtures + tests); no client names in the diff (demo fixtures + synthetic test factories only); copy rules on every new UI string (no em dashes, sentence case, plain words); lime rule intact; committed NOT pushed. The full sign-off table is in the report for Michael's word-level review; every cell is a one-line edit.

### Prior entry — The toast fix: deploys must announce themselves
- THE FINDING, restated: the B2b new-version toast was never BROKEN, it was UNREACHABLE, and both layers had to be true for the silence. (1) `public/sw.js` shipped a hardcoded `SW_VERSION = 'fox-s9-v1'` as a static asset, so every deploy served byte-identical bytes; a browser decides "there is a new worker" by byte-comparing the fetched sw.js against the installed one, so `updatefound` and `controllerchange` — the ONLY events the toast can listen for — could never fire. (2) Nothing asked: `ServiceWorkerRegister` never called `reg.update()`, and Next's client-side routing never triggers the browser's own check, so an idle tab waited on the ~24h check. The B2b tests were green throughout because they tested the DECISION RULES (unchanged, still correct); the rules were answering a question the browser never asked.
- TASK 1 (per-deploy identity): `git mv public/sw.js worker/sw.js` — a TEMPLATE now, not a served asset, carrying `const SW_VERSION = '__SW_VERSION__';`, deliberately kept a real .js file (the caching posture is the product: it stays lintable/diffable, not a string in a .ts module). NEW `lib/sw-source.ts` reads it once at module load and `swBody(version)` stamps it, THROWING on a missing placeholder or empty version — under the build-time route a throw FAILS THE BUILD instead of shipping a mute worker (the exact regression this module exists to prevent). NEW `app/sw.js/route.ts` (`dynamic = 'force-static'`) serves it at the UNCHANGED root path as text/javascript with `public, max-age=0, must-revalidate`. `deployVersion()`: VERCEL_GIT_COMMIT_SHA truncated to 12 (platform-provided — NO new env var) → BUILD_TIME (already baked by next.config, moves every build) → stable `dev` literal (a dev server must not churn). A redeploy of the SAME commit is the SAME version by design.
- TASK 2 (the triggers): `lib/sw-update.ts` += `UPDATE_CHECK_INTERVAL_MS` (10 min) + `scheduleUpdateChecks(check, host)` with an INJECTED host (timers/visibility listener/visibility read) because this repo has no jsdom — that constraint is why the triggers are pure and node-testable. `ServiceWorkerRegister` calls `reg.update()` once after registration settles, on the interval, and on `visibilitychange` when visible; teardown clears both; `cancelled` guards a late registration. The decision rules, the first-install guard, the toast markup, and NEVER-AUTO-RELOAD are untouched — a check only ASKS, the rules decide, the human presses Refresh.
- FINDINGS: (a) STATIC_CACHE already rode SW_VERSION and the activate handler already deleted every cache != current — the brief's "add old-cache cleanup if missing" was ALREADY SATISFIED (verified live: after a version change exactly one cache remained). (b) Clerk's middleware matcher `'/((?!.+\\.[\\w]+$|_next).*)'` excludes any path with a file extension, so `/sw.js` needs NO publicRoutes entry and Clerk never 401s it. (c) KNOWN GAP, confirmed by all three refuters and recorded rather than fixed (the brief scoped the decision wiring out): a HARD-RELOADED tab is uncontrolled (the navigation bypasses the worker), and all three toast paths use "has a controller" as a proxy for "a worker was already installed" — so such a tab is misread as a first install and misses ONE deploy. Bounded: the failure is SILENCE not a false toast, and it self-heals once claim() gives the tab a controller. The false premise ("no controller = the very first install") is corrected in the lib/sw-update.ts docstring; the real fix (compare registration.active identity vs the new controller) is handed to B5. (d) `self.skipWaiting()` in install is LOAD-BEARING for the Refresh button: the new worker activates immediately, so a reload lands on new code; it also means the `waiting` path is rarely the one that fires — the `updatefound → installed with a controller` path is.
- VERIFIED, PROVEN NOT ASSUMED: tsc clean; `next build` green; **647 tests** (from 634: +13 — interval/visibility/teardown proofs with fake timers, two-identities-differ + no-placeholder-left + refuses-a-hardcoded-version, `deployVersion` fallbacks; two deliberate updates — tests/pwa.test.ts repointed to worker/sw.js, pins the PLACEHOLDER instead of the old hardcoded version, and gained a served-bytes posture proof). The route is genuinely static, read off the BUILD OUTPUT: `.next/server/app/sw.js.body` exists carrying a stamped version, `/sw.js` is in the prerender manifest, `.meta` carries the custom headers — so the fs read happens at build, never at request time; belt-and-braces, `route.js.nft.json` traces `../../../../worker/sw.js`. TWO CONSECUTIVE BUILDS SHIPPED DIFFERENT BYTES (build-20260717125545 vs …125634) — the fix, demonstrated. Tree clean after a local build. Live dev: GET /sw.js 200, text/javascript, stamped, no placeholder, guard intact, scope `/`, cache `fox-static-<version>`. THE MECHANISM RAN END TO END LOCALLY: restarting the dev server mints a new BUILD_TIME (a genuine stand-in for a deploy) and THE REAL TOAST APPEARED in an open tab with exactly ONE cache remaining (screenshotted). The component's own wiring proven live with the real `update()` instrumented: exactly 1 call on a visible `visibilitychange`, 0 while hidden. NEAR-MISS WORTH KEEPING: the first instrumented run read 0 calls and looked like a bug — the tab was genuinely a background tab (`visibilityState: 'hidden'`) and the guard was correctly refusing. The harness was wrong, not the code; this is exactly the class of thing that looks correct and ships mute.
- CONFIRMED IN PRODUCTION (deploy e6d7aa6, pushed same session): https://foxmortgage.ca/sw.js and www both return 200 / text/javascript / `const SW_VERSION = 'sha-e6d7aa614a1d';` — the deploy's own commit, so layer 1 is proven live AND the PRIMARY identity path works (VERCEL_GIT_COMMIT_SHA is genuinely exposed at build; the review had flagged an absent SHA as a risk — it is not one, and BUILD_TIME stands behind it anyway). **THE REMAINING ACCEPTANCE IS THE NEXT DEPLOY, and this entry does not claim it:** an open, untouched portal tab should show the toast within about ten minutes, with no reload. Everything above proves the mechanism; only production proves the feature. THE LESSON, standing: unit tests proved the decision logic while the production triggers were unreachable — a feature of this class is only proven by a live deploy.
- Guardrails held: presentation only (no fetcher/gate/authz/data change; next.config.js and middleware.ts untouched, so the census and the seven redirects are untouched by construction); NO new env vars; no caching-behaviour change beyond version rotation + the pre-existing cleanup; never auto-reloads; no UI copy changed; committed NOT pushed.

- `config/lifecycle.ts` is the single source: LIFECYCLE_PHASES (five, plain-words descriptions), `phaseForDisplayStage` (derives from PIPELINE_STAGE_ORDER POSITIONS — segment boundaries, never a restated stage list; funded terminals → beyond_funding; non-funded terminals + unknown → null, LOUD), `phaseForBoardColumn` (total over the 7 keys), `boardPhaseGroups`, `groupByPhase` (lifecycle order + trailing loud 'Phase not mapped' group), `PHASE_STEPS` (per phase per shape purchase/refi/renewal/switch/unknown; every step {label, status live|manual|planned, note} — manual notes name the manual path so the dashboard doubles as the SOP; the two mandated planned placeholders are intake 'Application chase' and purchase-only 'Pre-approved · shopping'; planned steps carry NO stage matchers, tested — a missing capability is never where a file is), `stepShapeFor` (the house dealShapeOf, never re-derived; the Finmo goal wins a conflict, the header chip's own honesty rule; switch folds into renewal today), `journeyForStage`.
- BOARD (/portal/admin/underwriting): the SAME seven columns render under four phase section headers (small uppercase + hairline, ink, no lime); labels relabelled per the model (keys byte-stable); xl keeps the 1:3:1:2 relative widths, below xl the board stacks phase by phase (better mobile read than the old arbitrary 7-column wrap). Clutter pass: the page sub-line shortened to one sentence; a single-column phase no longer repeats its own name under the phase header (the INTAKE/INTAKE stack — the count stays); h1 to the shell face. Card contents byte-identical.
- TODAY: the compact pipeline table groups rows under quiet phase group rows (group rows over per-row chips — a chip would put a second pill on every row and repeat each phase word per file; groups say it once, in the board's words); closing-date order holds within groups; an unmapped stage lands under an amber 'Phase not mapped' group row; the pacing card's weight-unmapped flag is untouched.
- DEAL ROOM: `components/admin/JourneyStepper.tsx` (server component, display only, zero reads) mounts under the title strip — five phases (muted ✓ past / ink-navy current with the phase description as the title / gray future), the current phase's steps beneath (current step ink; manual + planned gray with a ◦ marker and the note on hover), funded files show Beyond funding + an 'Open renewals' link, an unmapped stage renders the loud amber state. Rendered for active rooms and funded rooms; a dormant/closed room shows no journey (a dead file has no current phase). Header sweep: both h1s + the back link to the shell face and ink tokens, back link renamed '← Underwriting', and 'Find rates for this deal' lost its bg-lime (a nav affordance is not a queued decision) for a quiet secondary style.
- CENSUS (the Task 0 anchor; live before/after BYTE-IDENTICAL, diff-proven; counts only): board 7 live rooms = intake 4 / evidence 1 / conditions 1 / funded 1 / others 0, parked 0; Today 8 active files = Application Started 2 / Underwriting In Progress 1 / Submitted to Lender 1 / Approved 3 / Ready To Close 1; unmapped-weight list = Ready To Close alone, before and after.

Render proofs on synthetic fixtures via a temp public /preview-b1 route (screenshotted, then route deleted + middleware entry reverted — middleware diff-zero). Report: docs/lifecycle-b1-2026-07-16.md, incl. the read-only visual debt inventory (72 of 81 admin files off-token: 150 legacy lime / 728 legacy navy / 1,321 raw grays / 1,010 font-heading|body classes — the B2/B3 feed).

### 2026-07-16 — Length-aware note + goal-conflict header honesty (small session)
- **The lender-notes card shows an over-length draft labelled for a manual trim instead of nothing.** The workbench length gate is now recoverable: a draft that passes the style + pinned-figure gates but exceeds the 3,750 character ceiling comes back flagged (`overCeiling` + `chars`), and the card shows "over the 3,750 ceiling, not auto-trimmed; the figures are validated, only the length is not; trim ~N characters by hand". `lib/lender-notes-client.ts` surfaces `overCeiling`/`chars` (only when over); `lib/gates.ts` type extended. A normal note is unchanged.
- **The deal-room header stops presenting the deal-record type as fact when it conflicts with the Finmo goal.** NEW `lib/deal-goal.ts` `dealGoalDisplay(dealType, finmoGoal)` (shape mirror of the workbench `dealShapeOf`; conflict only when BOTH are KNOWN and DIFFERENT). On a conflict the header chip shows the FINMO GOAL in amber with a marker ("Refinance · record says Purchase", tooltip names both + says correct in Zoho); else the record type as before. Display only — the deals row is Michael's to fix in Zoho. Verified: F053107 (record purchase vs Finmo refinance) renders the amber conflict chip. Portal **565 tests** green, `next build` green, tsc clean. See fox-underwriting CLAUDE.md "Length-aware generation + goal-conflict header honesty".

### 2026-07-16 — Lender notes: fresh pull + stale-snapshot second click (workbench 0048)
- **The Generate Lender Notes flow now ALWAYS pulls the current Finmo application server-side before building the note** (workbench Step 1); there is no freshness window. If the fresh pull fails, the workbench returns a loud `502` whose message ends "…confirm the stale-snapshot fallback", and the card (`components/admin/LenderNotesCard.tsx`) offers an explicit second click, "Generate from the snapshot pulled N ago", which re-calls with `allow_stale_snapshot: true` — never silently stale. `lib/lender-notes-client.ts runLenderNotesGeneration` gained `allowStale` and surfaces `staleFallbackAvailable` by detecting that marker; `lib/gates.ts generateLenderNotes` forwards `allow_stale_snapshot`; the proxy route passes it through. The readiness strip already shows the snapshot age.
- The note's figures are now deterministic and deal-type aware workbench-side (a refinance no longer renders a purchase/down payment; LTV is against the property value; a pinned-figure gate rejects a prose figure that contradicts the computed one). No portal rendering change — the note is text the card displays. Portal **559 tests** green, `next build` green, tsc clean. See fox-underwriting CLAUDE.md "The lender note reads the full application" and reports/refi-lender-note-2026-07-16.md.

### 2026-07-16 — Cross-repo note: refi payout fixed + the fresh pull actually persists (workbench, no portal code change)
- **Two production defects on `BRXM-F053107`, both workbench-side, both now fixed.** (1) The "always pull fresh before generating" step (the entry above) was SILENTLY not persisting: `pullFinmoAppSnapshot` set the `superseded_by` self-FK to a not-yet-inserted id, the UPDATE FK-failed unchecked, the re-insert hit the `one_current` index, and a fallback returned the STALE snapshot while reporting `refreshed`. It now supersedes → inserts → backfills the pointer with every error checked, so a generate grows the `finmo_app_snapshots` chain by one (proven live 1→2). (2) The note's DEBT RETIRED / NET PROCEEDS dropped the from-proceeds consumer debts (the stale `mapped` blob had no `liabilities` field); the note is now always built from the CURRENT mapper applied to the raw payload, and DEBT RETIRED = existing subject mortgage + every `payOffType==='from_proceeds'` liability, pinned.
- **Portal impact: none.** The note and its DEAL SNAPSHOT are workbench-generated text the card displays; the readiness strip's "pulled N ago" now reflects a chain that genuinely advances each generate. No portal code changed. See fox-underwriting CLAUDE.md "The refi payout was wrong and the fresh pull never fired" and reports/refi-payoff-and-fresh-pull-2026-07-16.md.

### 2026-07-15 — Cross-repo note: snapshot income/credit keyed by Finmo id (workbench 0047)
- **Workbench-side change (fox-underwriting), no portal code changed.** Follow-on to the borrower-identity fix: the Finmo application snapshot mapper now keys `mapped.incomes` and `mapped.credit` by the Finmo borrower id (each `MappedIncome`/`MappedCredit` gains `finmo_borrower_id`), not by name, so two same-named co-borrowers no longer merge their income/credit in the lender note (the file's two identically-named applicants now render as three distinct people). `finmo_app_snapshots.mapped` was re-derived for current snapshots (`npm run remap:snapshots`) — no schema change.
- **Portal impact: none today.** The only portal read of the snapshot (`getDealFinmoSnapshot` in `lib/underwriting.ts`, demo-guarded) uses `mapped.requested` only and renders no income/credit/borrowers. **If the portal ever renders `mapped.incomes`/`mapped.credit`, group by `finmo_borrower_id`, never by the display name** — two applicants on one deal can share a full name and are different people. See fox-underwriting CLAUDE.md "attributes income + credit by id" and reports/income-credit-attribution-2026-07-15.md.

### 2026-07-15 — Cross-repo note: borrower identity is the Finmo id (workbench migration 0046)
- **Workbench-side change (fox-underwriting), no portal code changed this note.** The workbench borrower unique key was `(deal_id, full_name, role)`, which collapsed two people who share a name+role into one row (deal `BRXM-F053107` had two identically-named co-borrowers and only two of three rows). Migration **0046** makes identity the **Finmo borrower id**: `borrowers` gains `finmo_borrower_id` (stable identity), `email`, `phone`, `relationship`, `identifiers_source`; the unique key becomes partial `(deal_id, finmo_borrower_id) where not null`. Same-named borrowers are now **distinct rows**.
- **What the portal must know:** the new columns are covered by the existing `portal_readonly` SELECT grant on `borrowers` (0026) — no new grant. **Anywhere the portal keys borrowers by name (a React key, a dedupe, a join), switch to `finmo_borrower_id`** — two borrowers on one deal can share a full name and are different people. `email`/`phone` are FULL in the row (masked in logs/reports); `relationship` is the structured kinship field per person. `zoho_contact_id` linking is now per-borrower by her own email (two same-named borrowers with distinct emails link to distinct contacts; a shared email stays unmatched).
- **Live status:** 0046 apply is a one-command follow-up (the Supabase MCP was unreachable from the workbench sandbox this session, `net::ERR_FAILED`); the fix is proven on the real F053107 data read-only (2 existing + 1 insert = 3 rows). Until 0046 lands, `borrowers.finmo_borrower_id` does not exist — a portal query selecting it would 400. See fox-underwriting CLAUDE.md "Borrower identity is the Finmo id" and reports/borrower-identity-2026-07-15.md.

### 2026-07-13/14 (night, addendum) — Stage guard, portal stage vocabulary, deal naming
- THE FINDING FIRST (contradicts the brief's parenthetical): the Deals Stage
  picklist carries DISPLAY/ACTUAL pairs that differ on five used stages, and
  Zoho READS (records API + COQL, everything the portal and the sync's
  searches consume) return the DISPLAY value while WRITES take the ACTUAL
  value. Verified live by getFields + a grouped COQL read ('Mortgage Funded'
  48, 'Conditionally Approved' 2, 'Underwriting In Progress' single-t 1 —
  all display forms). The brief said "portal reads must key on actual
  values"; the truth is inverted — the portal correctly keys on display, the
  sync correctly writes actual, and the GUARD was broken precisely because it
  compared the two spaces directly. The double-t typo is real and lives in
  the ACTUAL value only: 'Underwritting In Progress' (displays single-t).
  Pairs: 'Application Pending'→'Application Started', 'Underwritting In
  Progress'→'Underwriting In Progress', 'Application Sent To Lender'→
  'Conditionally Approved', 'Ready To Close'→'Broker Complete', 'Mortgage
  Closed'→'Mortgage Funded' (+ unused 'Closed Lost to Competition'→
  'Mortgage Lost').
- CHECK 1 (Finmo Sync v2, Build Deal Payload + Apply COQL Result; atomic ops
  + publish, active version 5fd50c60): STAGE_ORDER was 7 actual-space stages;
  the guard's unknown-current branch OVERWROTE — so every display-space read
  ('Conditionally Approved', 'Underwriting In Progress'…) and every hand-set
  stage outside the list (Collecting Documentation, legacy Options/Pending/
  Archive) fell to currentIdx===-1 and was clobbered by whatever Finmo
  mapped. Now: STAGE_ORDER = all 13 hand-settable stages (used picklist) in
  ACTUAL space, funnel-ordered by picklist probability, 'Underwritting In
  Progress' matched as stored, 'Mortgage Closed' last so funded history
  never rewrites; STAGE_READ_ALIASES canonicalizes display-space reads
  before comparison; an unknown NON-NULL current stage is PRESERVED with a
  loud 'Stage preserved (unknown to stage order)' error on Finmo_Sync_Error
  (null current still writes — the create path); borrower.update strips the
  new noise like the old. ADJACENT GAP fixed while in there: the COQL-confirm
  path (search-index lag) wrote dp.Stage UNGUARDED because Build Deal Payload
  had guarded against a null current stage — Apply COQL Result now re-runs
  the guard against the COQL-found live stage using the order/aliases
  EXPORTED from Build Deal Payload (_stageOrder/_stageReadAliases/
  _terminalStages — one definition, never duplicated).
- CHECK 3 (deal naming): create-only Deal_Name is now '{fileRef} — {primary
  borrower full name}' (space em-dash space; primary = isMainBorrower, else
  earliest-created, mirroring Extract Borrowers Array so the name matches
  Contact #1; bare reference when no borrower name). REPORT-ONLY finding:
  NO automated renamer exists — the fimoextension marketplace flow births
  deals already named (one file's creation event carries the full name), and
  bare sync-created ones were Michael's to fix by hand (the F059751 deal renamed
  crm_ui 22:03 tonight, no automation attached; workflow rules BLU023E/
  BLU055 fire on deal edits but never touch Deal_Name). One legacy stray:
  the F057400 deal name carries a plain hyphen, hand-typed variant.
- VERIFICATION (n8n side): a local harness ran the REAL node code (fetched
  back from n8n post-update, byte-identical to the reviewed files modulo a
  trailing newline) against 22 fixtures — the acceptance pair (Collecting
  Documentation + in_progress keeps its stage with the loud error; create
  with primary borrower → 'BRXM-FTEST01 — First Last'; without → bare ref),
  alias forward-motion ('Conditionally Approved' + approved → writes),
  alias protection ('Underwriting In Progress' + in_progress → kept),
  unknown-legacy preservation (Options + funded → kept + loud), terminal
  still writes, the backfill missed-create exemption intact (now with the
  named Deal_Name), COQL re-guard suppress/forward/error-as-not-found, and
  borrower.update noise-stripping. 22/22.
- CHECK 2 (portal, display space by design): STAGE_WEIGHTS gained
  'Submitted': 0.15 (re-enabled stage, probability 25, between Application
  Started .1 and Collecting Documentation .2) and 'Conditions Fulfilled':
  0.75 (probability 50, beside Conditionally Approved — an initial .8 was
